# Review log — 0100_fix_the-terminal-pipeline-what-happens-between-a-session-s

## Standing context

Changelog (compaction pass after spec round 8): the standing context was empty, so this pass lifted the ledger entries from spec.1 through spec.8. Corrections applied: the §29.4 renumbering is the round-7 layout (the step-16 prepend is withdrawn, see Traps); the usage.go retarget is step 13 rather than 16; the `pg_xact_status` check is replaced by `last_write_id`, and the old "did not file xid reuse" decision and the spec.4 xid UNVERIFIED and WATCHOUT are deleted; the SPEC-4(a) "latest checkpoint stands" clause is deleted and its WATCHOUT removed; the retention settle window is replaced by the GC guard; predicate-1 immediate reclaim is corrected by SPEC-6(b); the spec.6.review-security late-confirmed-write WATCHOUT is closed by the count-from in SPEC-1(a); the troubleshooting.md DEFERRED is closed by DOC-1; the spec.1.fix-design-G3.1 live-replica UNVERIFIED is closed by spec.2.review-performance. Duplicate checklist and docs DEFERREDs are merged per target, with every site kept. The repeated §29.7 indent note, grace-period math, and anchor-resolution facts are each kept once. The section is 133 lines, which meets the 200-line target. The ledger is left unedited for the round-boundary archive.

### Settled
- Owner's delegate, 2026-10-25: the end of the post-terminal window is observed through one durable release marker that the terminal pipeline's release writes (for example a release timestamp on the session row, or the release clearing `pod_assignment`), with TerminatedAt plus the terminal pipeline budget kept only as a crash cap. One store-level predicate built on it serves every consumer with one bound and one zero-value rule. A new column and its migration are permitted for this; the earlier 'no new store method, column, or migration' constraint from redesign.1 is lifted for this mechanism only. Do not reopen.

- **Terminal-first tree**: AfterTerminalWrite runs after the terminal Update commits and seals inside the hook; SPEC-1 is the first spec text to state terminal-first (spec.1.fix-G1.1, spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Hook order**: CancelResumeAttempt, settleStaleBinding, seal/failWorkspaceSeal, drainMessagingOnTerminal, status_change+session_complete, signals (cascadeOnFailure), releaseAfterTerminal (adapter Shutdown and lease return inside it), expireRaisedElicitations (spec.6.fix-design-G1.1).
- **§7.1 flow reorder (SPEC-1(b))**: steps 20-22 permuted in place: 20 terminal write, 21 seal + record artifact refs, 22 Shutdown; keeps step 23/24 numbers (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Step 23 citers**: §7.1 step 23 is cited from §7.2 (spec/07:223, :231) and §7.3 (:447); nothing cites steps 20-22; SPEC-1(b)'s "§7.3 cites step 23" holds (spec.4.review-citations, spec.6.review-citations).
- **§29.4 final layout (SPEC-1(c))**: 10 client, 11 terminal write (new), 12 seal (old 11), 13 drain/cascade/terminal events (new, `gateway`, `internal`), 14-16 Shutdown/FINAL_USAGE_REPORT/session_end (old 12-14), 17-19 lease/claim/return (old 16-18), old 15 deleted, intro "steps 10 through 19" (spec.6.fix-G1.1, spec.8.review-fresh).
- **§29.4 citers**: no spec/docs/schemas/tests site cites a §29.4 or §29.7 step number; step 10's "proceeds from step 11" still lands on the terminal write; the only code citer is usage.go:341 (see Deferred) (spec.8.review-edit-sites).
- **SPEC-1(d) CH-ATTACH Timing**: the §28.5.1 stream-end clause is SPEC-1(d), ending the stream at seal start with a pointer to §7.1 **Delivery and checkpoints during the seal:** (edit 6a) (spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **CH-ATTACH Degradation unchanged**: the §28.5.1 Degradation sentences and the §28.8 row stay true, because the eviction is a gateway-ended stream and EvictStream sets closedByGateway (spec.1.fix-design-G4.1, spec.2.review-edit-sites).
- **Budget count-from**: the terminal pipeline budget is counted from the terminal transition, stated once in SPEC-1(a); CODE-1 `terminalContext(ctx, terminatedAt)` gives the remainder on `s.clock`, full budget for zero `TerminatedAt` (spec.6.fix-G2.1).
- **TerminatedAt stamping**: MarkTerminated stamps inside the mutator before commit, keeps the first value on a relabel, and a store-retried mutator gets a fresh stamp (spec.6.fix-design-G2.1, spec.8.review-reliability).
- **Hook gets TerminatedAt**: AfterTerminalWrite and recordSessionCompleted receive `sess`, so `sess.TerminatedAt` needs no signature change (spec.6.fix-G2.1).
- **Relabel framing (SPEC-2(c))**: "relabels the session's committed terminal state as `failed` with reason `workspace_seal_timeout`" plus a sentence that the relabel takes no §7.2 edge and that terminal events, emitted after the seal ends, report the relabelled state when the relabel commits (spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.2.follow-up-fix).
- **No terminal-out edge in code**: ValidTransitions has no edge out of a terminal state; failWorkspaceSeal bypasses it without a generation bump (spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Failed relabel returns input row**: on a relabel Update error or a gone row, failWorkspaceSeal returns the input row and the hook emits from it (spec.2.follow-up-fix).
- **SPEC-2 parts**: (a)-(c) in the invariant, (d) §16.1 row, (e) §16.5 row, (f) §29.4 seal-step pointer; the §16.5 "Pods held in `draining` ... transitioned to `failed`" sentence is deleted (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **`permanent` widened**: covers an adapter permanent error, a binding lost during the seal, and a deleted session row; no new `lost_binding` label (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **§16.5 table authored**: the spec §16.5 table is hand-written; pkg/alerting/rules carries only `SpecRef` and generates docs/alerting/rules.yaml; rules_test compares PromQL only (spec.2.review-applicability, spec.6.review-docs-alignment).
- **Derive merge (SPEC-3(b))**: `completed`, `failed`, and `cancelled`/`expired` bullets merged into one sealed-else-checkpoint bullet; derive.go copies WorkspaceSnapshot for every terminal state (spec.1.fix-G1.1, spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Sealed vs checkpoint sources**: `sealed` and `checkpoint` are distinct snapshot sources (sessionstore.go:919-925) (spec.4.review-mechanism).
- **SPEC-4(a) home**: **Terminal pipelines at process exit.** is the one home of the shutdown-wait predicate "no terminal write, no terminal pipeline, and no release of a terminal session's binding is in progress on the replica"; §29.7 step 11 and the CODE-2 doc comment point at it (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **SPEC-4(a) checkpoint clause deleted**: "the latest successful checkpoint stands" removed; derive rule 1 is the one home of which snapshot stands (spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.4.fix-design-G1.1).
- **terminalClaimRecord scope**: size() counts writer claims (before the terminal Update to hook return), §11.4 full_revoke cancel-write claims until reclaim ends, and ReleaseTerminalBinding/ReleaseRevokedBinding marks; suspension releases (inflightSet) are not counted (spec.1.fix-design-G2.1).
- **Code-only vocabulary**: "terminal-writer claim", "terminal binding release", "primary-read bound", "release allowance", and "terminal pipeline" are absent from spec/; spec "claim" means SandboxClaim; SPEC-1 introduces "terminal write" (spec.1.review-* lenses).
- **`session.force_terminated`**: named nowhere in spec/ or docs/; exists in pkg/observability/audit/catalog.go:148, admin/sessions.go:23, and schemas/ocsf-mapping.yaml:327 (spec.1.review-security, spec.8.review-edit-sites).
- **Retention GC guard (F-7.1.28)**: `eligible` requires TerminatedAt plus the tier retention window (`RetentionFor`, which returns `retentionForTier`) to have elapsed and the row to be outside its post-terminal window (CODE-7 items 1 and 5), replacing the `SettleWindow`; SPEC-3(a)'s "It collects no artifact ... before any collection." is deleted (spec.1.fix-G3.1, spec.1.fix-design-G3.1, D22).
- **retentiongc callers**: `retentiongc.New` has one production caller (cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go:136, `w.sessionSrv` available) and test callers retentiongc_test.go and sweep_tenant_test.go, the latter named in TEST-1 item 9 (spec.1.fix-G3.1, spec.1.follow-up-fix).
- **Retention roll best-effort**: rollRetentionOnTerminal runs in emitTerminalLifecycle after the seal and discards its Update error (spec.1.fix-design-G3.1).
- **Create-time deadline stamping**: only create.go:276 and create_and_start.go:332 stamp RetentionExpiresAt; delegation, translator single-shot, mcptools, replay, and derive Create paths do not (spec.1.fix-design-G3.1).
- **SPEC-5 pointer**: the SPEC-5 row's misclassified-write clause points at §10.1 **Terminal pipelines at process exit.**; every "bounded by claimOrphanTimeout" restatement was cut (spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **SPEC-5 discriminator**: a per-update random `sessions.last_write_id` uuid (`$57` in updateSQL) read back from the primary after a commit error, replacing `pg_xact_status` (spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.4.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Single UPDATE site**: pgstore.Store.Update holds the only `UPDATE sessions` in pkg/ and cmd/; `s.pool` is the primary pool; uuid is a direct dependency; migrations latest 0188 (spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.6.review-reliability).
- **No sessions column list in spec**: no spec/docs/schemas text enumerates sessions columns (§4 "Session records (...)" is illustrative), so `last_write_id` needs no spec edit (spec.5.review-mechanism, spec.6.review-edit-sites).
- **Concurrent overwrite of last_write_id**: misclassifies as not applied, which the row states and which fails closed; not filed (spec.5.review-reliability, spec.6.review-mechanism).
- **Live-replica misclassification**: coordination sweepTerminal hands a terminal row this replica binds to ReleaseTerminalBinding on its next tick; until then the Checkpointer still checkpoints it (spec.2.review-performance).
- **Orphan predicates**: §4.6.1 predicates 1 and 3 count a session that holds its pod (§7.1 **Post-terminal window:** rule 2), with `claimOrphanTimeout` as the controller's cap; the lookup is the unchanged `GetActiveSlotsByPod` on a store built with that cap (SPEC-6 (b), CODE-7 item 5, D22).
- **Pod reclaim timing**: after SPEC-6(b) a pod left by a cut pipeline or a misclassified write becomes reclaimable `claimOrphanTimeout` after the terminal transition, matching the slot reaper; this corrects the spec.6.review-reliability "reclaimable at once" fact (spec.8.fix-G1.1).
- **SessionLookup unchanged**: its signature and its fakes (gc_test.go, gc_internal_test.go, gc_reclaim_internal_test.go, the tier8 chaos stubs) are not edited (CODE-7 item 5 (e)).
- **CODE-7 index row**: gains `migrations/`; summary "one-site fixes" became "fixes" (spec.8.fix-G1.1).
- **DOC-1**: is the docs/operator-guide/troubleshooting.md `SandboxClaimOrphanRateHigh` drain-condition edit, indexed in the summary and Files touched (spec.8.follow-up-fix).
- **Grace vs budget**: gateway grace 240 s at Tiers 1-2 and 300 s at Tier 3, both below the 330 s default budget (300 s seal + 30 s allowance); claimOrphanTimeout default 900 s gives 570 s spare (spec.1.review-citations, spec.8.review-performance).
- **Release allowance**: the existing `--stream-failure-report-timeout`, default 30 s (DefaultStreamFailureReportTimeout); not rendered by the chart (spec.1.review-reliability, spec.6.review-citations).
- **§11.3 not exhaustive**: the timeouts table omits maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds, so no release-allowance row is owed (spec.6.review-fresh, spec.8.review-edit-sites).
- **§15.1 terminate**: already yields `completed` from `resume_pending`; `resuming` is internal; SPEC-7 needs no §15.1, MCP, A2A, or §29 row edit (spec.1.review-client-surface).
- **No served schema change**: `workspaceSnapshotSource` is absent from openapi.json and sdks (pre-existing); extend-retention is only `ExtendRetentionRequest` (spec.4.review-client-surface, spec.6.review-client-surface).
- **Legal hold**: excluded at the GC SELECT, independent of any retention deadline (spec.4.review-security).
- **Credential lease on cut pipeline**: left to its TTL; not a security regression (spec.8.review-security).
- **Slot cleanup**: the seal-failure slot form lands on §5.2 **Slot cleanup:**, which removes workspace, credentials, and lease timers (spec.8.review-security).
- **Partial seal intent**: a crash-cut seal's partial intent row is reclaimed by the §12.5 backstop sweep (spec.4.review-reliability).
- **Plan conformance**: P2a's only spec deliverable is SPEC-1; DLQ/inbox drain and budget-expiry `expiryReason` (F-11.2.32, TT-88) are P2b; TT-29 off-holder is deferred in REC-1 item 5; F-4.9.33's terminal-writer half is declined (spec.6.review-plan-conformance).
- **Claim register**: tests/claim-map.json covers §28 statements only and is non-spec; no row owed for the §10.1.7/§12.3 departures (spec.1.review-plan-conformance).
- **Single-source judgments**: SPEC-7's multi-site edits follow pre-existing duplication; SPEC-6's budget-sum parenthetical is a gloss with citation; the §15.1 derive row is a summary; none filed (spec.8.review-single-source).
- **SPEC-1 "the same bound"**: §28.5.1 gives no stream-failure report bound, so SPEC-1 is its only statement (spec.8.review-single-source).
- **Not filed**: pool admission blind to the release allowance; §11.2.1 billing timing; the CODE-2 wait-return race and a pipeline opened during the wait; the sync-rep interrupted-commit hole (pre-existing) (spec.1.review-edit-sites, spec.4.review-*, spec.6.review-*).
- **Post-terminal window**: SPEC-1 (a) **Post-terminal window:** states it; dispositions are in [mechanism.post-terminal-window] as corrected by [mechanism.post-terminal-window-end].

### Traps

- **Step-16 prepend withdrawn**: rounds 1-5 prepended the old §29.4 step-15 drain/cascade/event sentence to the credential-lease step so 16-18 kept their numbers. That put the drain and events after Shutdown, FINAL_USAGE_REPORT, and session_end, against §29.1 **Step numbering.** and SPEC-1(a). Round 6 gave it its own step 13. Do not move it back (spec.6.fix-G1.1, spec.6.review-mechanism).
- **Single renumber owner**: SPEC-1(c)'s first paragraph renumbers only current step 11 to 12; the second paragraph owns every later renumber. Keep the two from both renumbering the same steps. Any added §29.4 step must also change the intro "steps 10 through N" (spec.6.fix-G1.1, spec.6.fix-design-G1.1).
- **pg_xact_status withdrawn**: rounds before 4 staged `pg_current_xact_id()`/`pg_xact_status($1::xid8)` and relied on "an xid from a lost primary reads as in the future". An xid8 is unique only within one server's durable history: a promoted standby or a crash-restarted primary can reassign it, so the check can report another transaction as committed. "Answer from a different server counts as unreadable" fails because no SQL identity separates the cases (system_identifier shared, timeline unchanged on crash restart, postmaster start time survives restart_after_crash). xmin has the same hazard. Do not reintroduce (spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.4.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Summary rejected-alternatives line**: it once rejected a write-token column because "Postgres reports the commit status directly (D15)"; it now rejects `pg_xact_status` and `updated_at` only (spec.4.fix-design-G1.1, spec.4.follow-up-fix).
- **No terminal→failed edge**: do not add a §7.2 or §6.2 terminal→failed edge for "seal failure overwrites a committed terminal state" or "GET shows completed then failed". The relabel is the answer, stated once in the invariant; an edge would cascade into §15.1 "Exactly one event per session", §8.8, docs diagrams, and ValidTransitions (spec.1.fix-design-G1.1, spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Events not unconditionally failed**: do not state that terminal events always report `failed`; a relabel that does not commit leaves the input row's state. CODE-4 item 8 changes only the release disposition (spec.2.follow-up-fix).
- **Shutdown predicate wording**: do not narrow the §10.1 predicate to "no terminal pipeline is open" (the claim opens before the write and also covers releases outside pipelines), and do not restate it in §29.7 step 11. One spec.1.fix-design-G2.1 variant read "a terminal session or a session whose row no longer exists"; the applied text is "a terminal session's binding" (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **CODE-2 doc comment vocabulary**: WaitTerminalWork's comment stays in code vocabulary equivalent to the spec rule; do not rewrite either into the other. A test should assert the wait spans a full_revoke reclaim's release, not just the cancel write (spec.1.fix-design-G2.1).
- **No fixed GC settle constant**: the retention window is per-tier (T2 90 d, T3 deployer flag, T4 24 h), so no constant is correct. Alternatives rejected: a `retention_window` column (migration plus Create-path stamping), keeping the settle with a departure (fails open), shifting the deadline by now-CreatedAt (inflates extend-retention) (spec.1.fix-G3.1, spec.1.fix-design-G3.1).
- **Zero RetentionExpiresAt**: stays ineligible; the GC zeroes the field after collecting, so making zero eligible re-sweeps every collected session (spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **Displayed retention deadline**: a lost roll leaves the API `retentionExpiresAt` at the create-time value while GC retains until TerminatedAt + window; display-only (spec.1.fix-design-G3.1).
- **RetentionFor design review**: CODE-7 item 1 and TEST-1 item 9 were rewritten in the spec loop; the non-spec loop should review nil `w.sessionSrv` at wiring, tier-lookup cost per expired row, and a tier change after termination (spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **claimOrphanTimeout bounds only pod and slot**: earlier drafting framed every departure as "bounded by `claimOrphanTimeout`"; that backstop reclaims pod and slot only. Those restatements were cut (spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **No snapshot outcome in §10.1.7**: do not re-add a workspace-snapshot outcome; the seal runs first, so a SIGKILL in the release allowance lands after a completed seal (spec.4.fix-G1.1).
- **SPEC-5 row frozen**: do not edit the lost-ack row again for the window; rounds 1, 4, and 6 each raised it, and with the count-from in SPEC-1 "including its post-commit steps" holds (spec.6.fix-G2.1).
- **Clock mismatch**: terminal mutators stamp TerminatedAt from different clocks (sessionserver, watchdog, orphancleanup, admin `now`). Compute elapsed on `s.clock`, never `context.WithDeadline` on the row's absolute time; a fake clock ahead of a real stamp gets an expired context (spec.6.fix-G2.1, spec.6.fix-design-G2.1).
- **No skip-when-spent branch**: an expired context still takes the slot lock and the binder writes run detached; a cut Shutdown leaves a `leaked` member, the existing cut-release outcome (spec.6.fix-design-G2.1).
- **Stream-failure report context**: ReportAttachStreamFailure bounds from the report; its terminal write's hook re-bounds from TerminatedAt because WithoutCancel drops the outer deadline (spec.6.fix-design-G2.1).
- **Predicate 3 shares the phrase**: SPEC-6 (b) replaces it in predicates 1 and 3 with different text; anchor each replacement to its own list item.
- **Release clears no pod_assignment**: the §8.8 rollup (resultrollup.go:87), the §8.9 projection (sessionstore.go:830), and the admin view (admin/sessions.go:91) read `pod_assignment` on terminal rows; the marker is `pod_released_at` (D22).
- **SPEC-6(b) alternatives withdrawn**: re-stamping bindingStateTransitionTime at the terminal write (apiserver write per end, fails open), a separate SPEC-8, seal-before-terminal, and an accepted departure were rejected (spec.8.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Planned DOC-1 never existed**: the G4 design planned a DOC-1 for docs/api/internal.md that was never created; DOC-1 is now the troubleshooting edit, so the internal.md edit extends DOC-1 or takes DOC-2 (spec.1.fix-G4.1, spec.8.follow-up-fix).
- **Fixer grant**: the spec-loop fixer may not author new staged docs or tests in non-spec-changes.md; G1's DOC-1 and TEST-1 item 10 extension were reported in designRejected (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **§29.7 indent note**: SPEC-4(c) says §29.7 uses a three-space indent "matching steps 10 and earlier"; steps 1-9 use three, steps 10-11 four. Rendering-neutral; filed by no lens. §29.4 uses four. Follow the file (spec.1.fix-G1.1, spec.8.review-mechanism).
- **Wrapped anchors**: §28.5.1 Timing (spec/28:243-246), §29.4 seal step, and §29.7 step 11 wrap, so one-line `grep -F` misses them (spec.4.review-citations).
- **"seal" in CH-ATTACH**: the card already uses "seal" for the idle SealSession RPC; SPEC-1(d)'s §7.1 link disambiguates, and dropping it reopens the ambiguity (spec.4.review-fresh, spec.6.review-fresh).
- **force_terminated link**: SPEC-1 names `session.force_terminated` with a §11.7 link though §11.7 defines no such event; the fix is dropping the name or citing §24.11, never adding it to §11.7/§16.7. A round-8 lens read the link as pointing at the audit mechanism (spec.1.review-security, spec.8.review-edit-sites).
- **Circular labels**: SPEC-1 and SPEC-4(a) cite each other's new bold labels, so S1 lands a forward reference to S4's label (spec.1.review-applicability).
- **Failover read-back**: during a failover the 5 s read-back usually expires first, classifying lost-ack writes not applied; no worse than shipped (F-7.2.29); do not file (spec.6.review-performance).
- **Stage-3 stream poll**: §10.1.7 stage 3 polls streams for the whole grace, so the SPEC-4 wait can get zero time; stated as a departure (spec.1.review-fresh).
- **Pre-existing out-of-scope sites**: spec/24:137 force-terminate "transitions immediately to `failed`"; §29 off-holder route row omits `resuming`; failWorkspaceSeal comment says any failed row is skipped while code skips only failed+workspace_seal_timeout, and the spec never states that exception (spec.1.review-docs-alignment, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1, spec.3.review-mechanism).
- **REST terminate response row**: after a seal timeout, handleTerminalWrite returns the pre-hook `completed` row while the row is relabelled `failed`; not filed since §29.4 step 18 does not say which row is returned (spec.4.review-docs-alignment).
- **Terminology drift**: non-spec-changes.md:7, :156 and checklist S10 still say "commit-status" (spec.5.review-mechanism).

- DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md] S2: says "Lands the two **Seal-and-export invariant:** edits". SPEC-2 has items (a)-(c) in the invariant and (d)-(f) in §16.1, §16.5, and §29.4; S2 should name the parts without a count (spec.1.fix-G1.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1).
- DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md] S10: says "Lands the commit-status check in `pgstore.Store.Update`."; should read "Lands the write-identifier check in `pgstore.Store.Update` and its migration." The migration adding `sessions.last_write_id` must land before or with the pgstore change (spec.4.fix-G1.1).
- DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md] docs/api/internal.md step: a later docs deliverable for docs/api/internal.md also needs a step (Tiers 0, 11, depending on S1), and S15/S16 ordering should account for it (spec.8.follow-up-fix, spec.1.fix-design-G1.1, spec.1.fix-design-G4.1).
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md] metrics doc: docs/reference/metrics.md:350 says `permanent` is "a permanent adapter error". After SPEC-2(d) it is any condition the invariant classifies as permanent: an adapter permanent error, a binding lost during the seal, or a deleted session row. tests/tier11_docs/checkpoint_presigned_capability_doc_reconciliation_test.go:136 (and comments at :15, :116-122) pins the old phrase "failed on a permanent adapter error and returned immediately without retrying" and moves in the same deliverable. Keep "`success`, `timeout`, `permanent`" and "`outcome=\"timeout\"` arm alone" (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md] seal runbook: docs/runbooks/workspace-seal-stuck.md:29 says "The session cannot reach `completed` state until the seal succeeds". Under SPEC-1(b)/(c) the session holds its terminal state while the seal retries, and a failed seal relabels it `failed`/`workspace_seal_timeout`. Runbook :36 "permanent adapter error ... adapter logs" should name the three permanent conditions and the gateway logs. Check the runbook against SPEC-2(e) (spec.1.fix-G1.1, spec.8.review-edit-sites).

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-4(a) **Terminal pipelines at process exit.** is the one home of the shutdown-wait predicate: "no terminal write, no terminal pipeline, and no release of a terminal session's binding is in progress on the replica". §29.7 step 11 (SPEC-4(b)) and the CODE-2 `WaitTerminalWork` doc comment point at it — BECAUSE the spec defines neither "writer claim" nor "binding release" ("claim" reads as SandboxClaim), and the set must match what `terminalClaimRecord.size()` counts — ALTERNATIVES: pipeline-only predicate (drops the pre-commit write D5 covers and the CODE-5 ReleaseGone / §11.4 full_revoke releases); new defined term "terminal work" (one use); renaming the bold label (SPEC-1 cites it).
WATCHOUT: Do not narrow the §10.1 predicate to "no terminal pipeline is open". SPEC-1 starts the pipeline after commit, while the claim record counts from before the terminal Update (BeginTerminal) and also marks releases that run outside any pipeline. Do not restate the predicate in §29.7 step 11, which is a pointer by design. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replica_records.go:71-85 (terminalClaimEntry and the terminalClaimRecord doc)
FACT: After G1's edits, SPEC-4(a) sits at spec-changes.md:106 and §29.7 step 11 at :112, while the design quoted :75 and :81. — EVIDENCE: grep "Terminal pipelines at process exit" in the spec-changes file

### [spec.1.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: F-7.1.28 closed by a GC guard keyed on `TerminatedAt` plus the session's tier retention window (`retentiongc.Options.RetentionFor`, sessionserver `(*Server).RetentionWindow` wrapping `retentionForTier`), replacing the 330 s `SettleWindow` — BECAUSE `MarkTerminated` commits `TerminatedAt` inside every terminal write, so the guard survives SIGKILL/crash, a failed best-effort roll, and a misclassified commit, while a budget-length settle lets GC delete a long-lived session's artifacts ~330 s after termination — ALTERNATIVES: retention_window column + roll inside MarkTerminated (migration, and several Create paths stamp no deadline today); keep the settle and record a departure (fails open on user data); shift deadline by now-CreatedAt (inflates extend-retention); a GC sentence in SPEC-3(a) (second statement of "measured from the session's terminal transition").
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce a fixed-length settle constant for the GC. The window is per-tier (T2 90 d, T3 deployer flag, T4 24 h), so no constant is correct, and a cut pipeline leaves the create-time deadline already expired for a session that outlived its TTL. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/lifecycle.go:427-470 (roll is best-effort, result discarded; retentionForTier), pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/terminal_mark.go:42-43
WATCHOUT: do not make zero `RetentionExpiresAt` eligible to catch rows with no deadline; the GC zeroes the field after collecting, so that re-sweeps every collected session. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/retentiongc/retentiongc.go:145-150

DECISION: SPEC-5 row's last clause now points at §10.1 **Terminal pipelines at process exit.** for a misclassified committed terminal write — BECAUSE the claimOrphanTimeout backstops reclaim only pod and slot, and SPEC-4(a) is the one home of a cut pipeline's outcome; with the GC guard, retention is unaffected so SPEC-4(a) needs no retention clause — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's explicit list in the row (second statement of SPEC-4(a)).
MISTAKE: earlier drafting framed every departure as "bounded by `claimOrphanTimeout`" (spec-changes edge list, summary D19, REC-1 item 3, non-spec edge bullet). That backstop bounds only pod and slot capacity; all of those restatements were cut or pointed at SPEC-4/SPEC-5 this round.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]

Suspension releases live in inflightSet and are NOT counted. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replica_records.go:71-134, :151-160

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-4(a) predicate becomes "no terminal write, no terminal pipeline, and no release of a terminal session's binding is in progress on the replica"; §29.7 step 11 and the CODE-2 doc comment cite §10.1.7 **Terminal pipelines at process exit.** instead of restating it — BECAUSE the spec has no "writer claim" or "binding release" term ("claim" reads as SandboxClaim), and the wait must cover the pre-commit write (D5 budget terminator racing SIGTERM) and the releases outside any pipeline (CODE-5 ReleaseGone, §11.4 full-revoke release) that terminalClaimRecord.size() counts — ALTERNATIVES: "no terminal pipeline open" only (reviewer's example) drops the pre-commit write D5 exists for and makes size() a superset of the spec rule; a new defined term "terminal work" in SPEC-1 adds a term for one use; a code-side pipeline-only tracking set contradicts D6.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: F-7.1.28 / finding 6 closes in the retention GC: `eligible` requires the session's retention window (tier TTL via a `RetentionFor` callback wrapping sessionserver.retentionForTier) to have elapsed from `TerminatedAt`, whatever `RetentionExpiresAt` says; this replaces CODE-7's budget-length `SettleWindow`/`DefaultSettleWindow`, and SPEC-3(a)'s last sentence ("It collects no artifact ... before any collection.") is deleted — BECAUSE the roll (rollRetentionOnTerminal) is a best-effort store Update in the signal tail after the seal, so a cut pipeline, a failed roll, or a misclassified terminal write leaves the create-time deadline and a budget-length settle then lets GC collect early; a TTL-length guard on TerminatedAt (which MarkTerminated commits in the terminal write) is correct in every one of those cases with one changed predicate — ALTERNATIVES: (A) a `retention_window` column stamped at create and rolled inside MarkTerminated: needs a migration plus stamping on every Create path, and the delegation child, translator single-shot, mcptools, replay, and derive-failure Create paths do not stamp a deadline today; (C) keep the settle window and record a departure: no bounding fallback (artifacts of a session that outlived its TTL go ~330 s after termination), fails the plan's drafting rule; shifting the deadline by (now - CreatedAt) in MarkTerminated inflates a pre-terminal extend-retention.
DECISION: finding 7: the SPEC-5 row's misclassified-write clause becomes a pointer to §10.1.7 **Terminal pipelines at process exit.** (the outcome for a cut pipeline), and every "bounded by claimOrphanTimeout" restatement (spec-changes edge list, D19, REC-1 item 3, non-spec edge list) is cut — BECAUSE the claimOrphanTimeout backstops reclaim only the pod and slot, and SPEC-4 is already the one home of the cut-pipeline outcome — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's explicit outcome list in the row (second statement of SPEC-4's outcome).
FACT: retentiongc clears RetentionExpiresAt to zero after collecting a session, so zero must stay "ineligible"; any GC predicate that collects zero-deadline rows re-sweeps every collected session forever — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/retentiongc/retentiongc.go:264-270, :145-150
FACT: only create.go:276 and create_and_start.go:332 stamp a create-time RetentionExpiresAt; delegation/service.go:1546, translator/singleshot.go:114, mcptools_register.go:212, replay.go:220, derive.go:674 Create rows without one — EVIDENCE: grep 'RetentionExpiresAt *=' pkg cmd
FACT: rollRetentionOnTerminal runs inside emitTerminalLifecycle (signal tail), after the seal step of AfterTerminalWrite, and discards the Update result — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/lifecycle.go:254-274, :440-454; terminal_hook.go:71-86
WATCHOUT: with the GC guard, the API-visible retentionExpiresAt of a session whose roll was lost still shows the create-time deadline while GC retains artifacts until TerminatedAt + window; this is display-only and is not a collection defect — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/retention.go:97-100
UNVERIFIED: a committed terminal write misclassified by CODE-3 on a LIVE replica leaves the in-memory binding registered (unlike a crash); whether the Checkpointer keeps driving checkpoints for that terminal row and whether anything releases the binding before claimOrphanTimeout is unchecked — mechanism lens next round.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: stage the §28.5.1 CH-ATTACH **Timing.** edit as SPEC-1(b) (SPEC-1's existing insert becomes (a)), and mirror it in docs/api/internal.md as a new DOC-1 under "Staged docs changes" — BECAUSE the stream end at seal start is a consequence of the SPEC-1 no-delivery sentence, so it lands with it and cites §7.1 **Terminal pipeline context:** by heading rather than restating the interval — ALTERNATIVES: a separate SPEC-8 (adds a deliverable and a checklist step for one sentence); rewording SPEC-1 to say "the stream stays open but carries nothing" (false: CODE-4 item 5 evicts the stream so an in-flight turn ends, per summary D7).
DECISION: the §28.5.1 **Degradation.** sentence "A stream the gateway ends itself, when it releases the session's binding or discards its cached streams, is not a failure." and its docs mirror are left unedited — BECAUSE the preceding rule is scoped to "A stream the gateway did not end", so the seal-start eviction is already outside the failure rule, and EvictStream discards the cached stream; the sentence stays true.
FACT: docs/api/internal.md never uses the word "seal"; a docs mirror of the seal-start end must say "starts exporting the session's final workspace" rather than "seal". EVIDENCE: grep -i seal docs/api/internal.md (0 hits)
FACT: EvictStream sets closedByGateway under e.mu, so an eviction-caused end is never reported as a stream failure; no code change is needed for the Degradation rule. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:180-201, podstream.go:381-392

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the seal-outcome relabel stays a relabel with no §7.2 edge; SPEC-2(c) words it as relabelling the committed terminal state, and one sentence after the invariant's alert sentence states that it takes no §7.2 edge and that the terminal events, which the gateway emits after the seal ends, report `failed` — BECAUSE the tree models it exactly so: pkg/session/state/state.go ValidTransitions has no terminal-out edge, failWorkspaceSeal bypasses it, and AfterTerminalWrite emits status_change/session_complete after the seal on the relabelled row, which is what keeps §15.1 `terminated` "Exactly one event per session" true — ALTERNATIVES: a `completed|cancelled|expired → failed` §7.2 edge (cascades into §6.2, the §8.8 mirror sentence, §15.1 one-event and state_change rows, both docs state diagrams, and ValidTransitions; and it misdescribes the tree); dropping the relabel (client contract change outside P2a); reverting to seal-first (contradicts the terminal-first tree and SPEC-1); a second sentence at §7.2 "Terminal states:" (second home; §7.2 makes no finality claim to correct).

WATCHOUT: failWorkspaceSeal's doc comment says the relabel is skipped for any already-failed session, but the code skips only failed+workspace_seal_timeout, so a failed row with another failure class is relabelled. ### [spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-4(a) **Terminal pipelines at process exit.** loses the clause "the latest successful checkpoint stands as the session's workspace snapshot" — BECAUSE derive rule 1 (SPEC-3(b)) is the one home of which snapshot stands (sealed once the seal completed, else last checkpoint), and a cut after the seal leaves the sealed snapshot standing — ALTERNATIVES: a pointer to derive rule 1 (one rung higher, says nothing the rule does not); enumerating sealed-else-checkpoint in §10.1.7 (second home of derive rule 1).
WATCHOUT: do not re-add a workspace-snapshot outcome to §10.1.7; the seal runs first in the pipeline (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go seal before drain/events/releaseAfterTerminal), so a SIGKILL inside the release allowance lands after a completed seal — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:920-924 (WorkspaceSnapshotSealed vs WorkspaceSnapshotCheckpoint).
DECISION: SPEC-5 / CODE-3 discriminator is a per-update random `sessions.last_write_id` (uuid, `$57` in updateSQL) read back from the primary after a commit error that follows a successful Exec — BECAUSE an xid8 is unique only within one server's durable history: a promoted standby, or the same primary after a crash restart that lost unflushed WAL (timeline unchanged), can reassign a lost transaction's xid, so `pg_xact_status` can answer `committed` for an unrelated transaction and run terminal post-commit steps on a non-terminal row — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "answer from a different server counts as unreadable" (no SQL-visible identity covers crash restart: system_identifier shared by replicas, timeline unchanged on crash restart, pg_postmaster_start_time survives restart_after_crash); xmin (32-bit server-scoped xid, same hazard); LastWriteID in sessionstore.Session (widens selectList/scanSession/memstore for a pgstore-only read); delete CODE-3 and record a departure (reopens F-7.2.29 for every network blip).
MISTAKE: rounds before 4 staged `pg_current_xact_id()`/`pg_xact_status($1::xid8)` and relied on "an xid from a lost primary reads as in the future"; that holds only until the new primary hands out that many xids (sub-second at Tier 3). Cost: a design replacement across SPEC-5, CODE-3, TEST-1 item 5, REC-1, D15, D19, and the rejected-alternatives list.
CORRECTS [review-log.md "DECISION: did not file the pg_xact_status xid-reuse-after-failover concern on SPEC-5"]: the concern was real; xid reuse after failover or crash restart misclassifies in the unsafe direction. The mechanism is now `last_write_id`.
FACT: `updateSQL` in pgstore.Store.Update uses placeholders $1..$56 (last is accumulated_session_age_seconds), so the new column binds as $57; `github.com/google/uuid` is already imported; migrations are `migrations/NNNN_name.{up,down}.sql`, latest 0188 on this tree — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:491-529, :641-642, :22; migrations/0188_audit_redaction_receipts_drop_fk.up.sql.
FACT: no spec or docs text enumerates the sessions table columns or names pg_xact_status / "commit acknowledgement", so the new column needs no further staged spec edit — EVIDENCE: lookup exact over spec, docs, schemas returned 0 hits (2026-10-24).
DEFERRED [proposals/0100_fix_the-terminal-pipeline-what-happens-between-a-session-s/0100_fix_the-terminal-pipeline-what-happens-between-a-session-s.implementation-checklist.md]: S10 says "Lands the commit-status check in `pgstore.Store.Update`." CODE-3 now also stages a migration; S10 should read "Lands the write-identifier check in `pgstore.Store.Update` and its migration." If the checklist orders migrations separately, the migration must land before or with the pgstore change, because the UPDATE names the column.

### [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-4(a) cut-pipeline outcome: delete the clause "the latest successful checkpoint stands as the session's workspace snapshot," rather than pointing at derive rule 1 — BECAUSE no implementor builds anything from it; derive rule 1 (SPEC-3(b)) is already the one home of which snapshot stands (sealed once the seal completed, else checkpoint), and the SPEC-5 row's pointer to "the outcome ... for a cut pipeline" stays coherent (terminal state kept, pod and slot reclaimed) — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's pointer to derive rule 1 (one rung higher; a pointer whose only job is to say "the normal rule applies").
DECISION: SPEC-5 lost-ack check moves from `pg_xact_status(xid8)` to a per-write `sessions.last_write_id uuid` the UPDATE stores and the store reads back from the primary after a commit error — BECAUSE a transaction ID is server-scoped: a primary promoted by failover, or the same primary after a crash restart, can reassign an xid whose WAL never became durable, and a status check then reports another transaction as committed; a random write identifier exists on whichever primary answers only if this write committed there — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "answer from a different server counts as unreadable" (no SQL identity separates the cases: system_identifier is shared by physical replicas, the timeline does not change on an in-place crash restart, pg_postmaster_start_time survives restart_after_crash reinit, and pg_walfile_name timeline parsing is unavailable on some managed services); dropping CODE-3 and accepting every lost ack as a departure (reopens F-7.2.29 and F-12.8.56 for network blips and proxy restarts, not only failover).
WATCHOUT: summary's rejected-alternatives list carried "A write-token column ... Postgres reports the transaction's commit status directly (D15)". That reason is the one this finding refutes; the line inverts to reject the xid status check. — EVIDENCE: summary.md:83
FACT: pgstore `s.pool` is the primary pool (GetPrimary reads it; Get reads the replica pool); updateSQL's highest placeholder is $56 — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:426-432, :491-529
FACT: migrations live at repo-root migrations/NNNN_name.{up,down}.sql, latest 0188 as of 2026-10-24; github.com/google/uuid is a direct dependency — EVIDENCE: migrations/0188_audit_redaction_receipts_drop_fk.up.sql, go.mod:35
CORRECTS [spec.2 review-log ledger "did not file the pg_xact_status xid-reuse-after-failover concern"]: the hazard is not limited to unreplicated heap WAL at failover; an in-place crash restart also reassigns xids whose WAL was not flushed, and the check's 5 s window (DefaultPrimaryReadTimeout) is long enough for a fast restart. Two verifiers confirmed the finding this round.
UNVERIFIED: whether a migration test enumerates `sessions` columns or pins a schema snapshot that a new `last_write_id` column breaks; the implementor of CODE-3 should grep migrations/*_test.go and tests/ for a sessions column inventory.
DEFERRED [proposals/0100_fix_the-terminal-pipeline-what-happens-between-a-session-s/0100_fix_the-terminal-pipeline-what-happens-between-a-session-s.implementation-checklist.md]: S10 says "Lands the commit-status check in `pgstore.Store.Update`". After this round CODE-3 is the write-identifier check plus a migration adding `sessions.last_write_id`, so S10 should read "Lands the write-identifier check in `pgstore.Store.Update` and its migration."

### [spec.4.review-reliability.1]
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5's commit-status check (pg_xact_status on the xid8 recorded in the transaction) is not failover-safe. Under the §12.3 synchronous-replication topology, a commit flushed only on the old primary is lost at promotion, and the new primary can hand out that same xid8 to an unrelated transaction. A status query that reaches the new primary after it has advanced past that xid then reads `committed` for someone else's transaction. CODE-3's "an xid from a lost primary reads as in the future" is true only until the new primary catches up. The fix is to pin the check to the server or timeline that ran the transaction. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:140; non-spec-changes.md:87-89; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:30
FACT: the stale slot release and the orphaned-claim Live-binding predicate both key on "no active session references the pod" or "terminal for longer than claimOrphanTimeout". They reclaim a cut terminal pipeline's pod and slot even when the replica that cut it is still alive, so they cover the SPEC-5 misclassified-commit case and not only the SIGKILL case. Credential leases have a TTL (leaseTTLSeconds), so a cut pipeline does not leak one indefinitely. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:591, spec/04_system-components.md:1274
FACT: a crash-cut seal leaves a partial intent row that the §12.5 `partial = true AND deleted_at IS NULL AND terminal_state` backstop sweep reclaims, so SPEC-4(a) needs no cleanup clause for it. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:363, spec/10_gateway-internals.md:151
DECISION: did not file the race where the CODE-2 shutdown wait returns at its last zero observation and a background loop then opens a terminal claim before Close() — BECAUSE the cut pipeline gets the same orphan-GC outcome as a SIGKILL cut, and verifiers would refute it as hardening — ALTERNATIVES: filing a SPEC-4 reordering that stops background terminal writers before the wait.

FACT: The SPEC-4 crash bound (orphaned-claim drain, stale slot member Shutdown after claimOrphanTimeout) is the existing §4.6.1 and §5.2 mechanism; the proposal adds no new reuse path. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:515, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:591

### [spec.6.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1(c) puts the old step 15 drain/cascadeOnFailure/terminal-event sentence in its own new §29.4 step 13 (`gateway`, `internal`), directly after the seal step 12 and before `Shutdown` (now 14). Current 12-14 become 14-16, current 16-18 become 17-19, and the intro's "steps 10 through 18 trace it" becomes "steps 10 through 19 trace it" — BECAUSE §29.1 **Step numbering.** numbers steps in the order they occur, and AfterTerminalWrite seals, drains, emits events, runs signals (cascadeOnFailure), and only then calls releaseAfterTerminal, inside which adapter Shutdown is issued — ALTERNATIVES: fold into step 11 (drain before seal, contradicts SPEC-2(c)); fold into seal step 12 (a `gateway` → `adapter` hop); 13a/13b letter suffixes (§29.1 allows them only for unfixed order); keep it in the credential-lease step (round 5's choice, three steps late).
MISTAKE: round 5 kept the moved sentence in old step 16 (credential lease) so steps 16-18 kept their numbers. That placed the drain and terminal events after Shutdown, FINAL_USAGE_REPORT, and session_end, which contradicts §29.1 and SPEC-1(a). It cost round 6 a finding.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-1(c)'s first paragraph used to say "renumber the current steps 11 to 14 as 12 to 15". With the new step 13 that would have given two renumberings of current step 12. It now says "renumber the current step 11 as 12", and the second paragraph owns every later renumber. Keep the two paragraphs from both renumbering the same steps — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1 (c)
FACT: The §29.4 order after SPEC-1(c) is: 11 terminal write, 12 seal, 13 drain/cascade/terminal events, 14 Shutdown, 15 FINAL_USAGE_REPORT, 16 session_end, 17 credential lease, 18 pod release, 19 client. Step 10's "proceeds from step 11" and SPEC-2(f)'s "seal step (renumbered by SPEC-1 (c))" stay true — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:66-91; spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:59-62, :611, :711-783
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:341 (terminalSignals doc) cites "the cascadeOnFailure evaluation of §29.4 step 15". After SPEC-1(c) that evaluation is in §29.4 step 13. Cite the §29.4 heading or step 13. This CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G1.1]'s DEFERRED, which said step 16.

### [spec.6.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: The terminal pipeline budget is counted from the session's terminal transition, stated once in SPEC-1 (a) ("The **terminal pipeline budget**, counted from the session's terminal transition, bounds ..."). CODE-1 `terminalContext(ctx, terminatedAt)` gives the pipeline only the remainder of the budget measured on `s.clock`, and a zero stamp gets the whole budget. — BECAUSE the §5.2 stale slot release ages a member from `terminated_at` (slotreaper.go cutoff `Now().Add(-TerminalAge)`), and `MarkTerminated` stamps that value inside the mutator before commit (sessionstore/terminal_mark.go:37-47). With an entry anchor, every lag between the stamp and the hook entry pushes the pipeline past the window. That lag covers ordinary commit latency, writer work before the hook, and the SPEC-5 commit stall plus primary read-back. — ALTERNATIVES: adding a remaining-budget qualifier to the SPEC-5 row (the third local exception to that row, and the ordinary commit-latency lag stays open); adding the count-from to SPEC-6 (a second home, and SPEC-4 and CODE-1 read SPEC-1); raising the claimOrphanTimeout floor by the primary-read bound (the commit stall runs on the writer's unbounded context); skipping the pipeline when nothing remains (an extra branch the cut path already covers).
WATCHOUT: Do not edit the SPEC-5 lost-ack row again for this window. Rounds 1, 4, and 6 each raised it, and since the count-from now lives in SPEC-1 its "including its post-commit steps" is true as written. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5 §12.3 durability row; SPEC-6 cites SPEC-1 **Terminal pipeline context:**.
WATCHOUT: Terminal mutators stamp `TerminatedAt` from several clocks. The sessionserver uses `s.clock`, the watchdog `w.clock`, orphancleanup `s.clock`, and admin/cmd use a local `now`, all wall time in production. A hook test whose fake `s.clock` runs ahead of a real-time stamp gets an expired context. The `min(budget, budget-elapsed)` cap only protects a clock that runs behind (negative elapsed). — EVIDENCE: grep `MarkTerminated(` across pkg/ and cmd/; sessionserver.go:1824 default `s.clock`.
FACT: The hook entries receive `sess sessionstore.Session` (terminal_hook.go:66 `AfterTerminalWrite(ctx, fromState, sess, signals)`; usage.go:331 `recordSessionCompleted(ctx, fromState, sess)`), so `sess.TerminatedAt` reaches both without a signature change. CODE-3's confirmed `out` is the mutated row and carries the stamp.

### [spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1(c) gives the moved drain/cascade/terminal-event sentence its own `gateway`, `internal` step 13 between the seal (12) and `Shutdown` (14), and the intro range becomes "steps 10 through 19". BECAUSE AfterTerminalWrite runs seal, then drain, then terminal events, then signals (cascadeOnFailure), then releaseAfterTerminal, and `Shutdown` is issued inside the release. SPEC-2(c) also makes "terminal events after the seal ends" normative. ALTERNATIVES: appending the sentence to the new terminal-write step 11 was rejected because it would put the drain and events before the seal, which contradicts SPEC-2(c) and the hook. Appending it to the seal step 12 was rejected because that step is a `gateway` → `adapter` hop and a step names one hop. A lettered pair with Shutdown/FUR/session_end was rejected because the tree fixes the order.
FACT: In AfterTerminalWrite the order is CancelResumeAttempt, settleStaleBinding, sealWorkspace/failWorkspaceSeal, drainMessagingOnTerminal, emitStatusChange+emitSessionComplete, signals, releaseAfterTerminal, expireRaisedElicitations. Adapter Shutdown and the credential-lease return both happen inside the release. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:66-89; podsession/release.go:191; non-spec-changes.md:118
WATCHOUT: When a §29.4 step is added, the intro sentence's "steps 10 through N" (spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:611) must change in the same edit. SPEC-2(f) locates the seal step by "(renumbered by SPEC-1 (c))" rather than by number, so it stays correct.
OPEN: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:340-341 cites "cascadeOnFailure evaluation of §29.4 step 15". SPEC-1(c) already makes that citation stale, and after this fix the content sits in step 13. No non-spec deliverable retargets it. This is a separate non-spec finding: cite the §29.4 heading only, or the drain step.

### [spec.6.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: count the terminal pipeline budget from the session's terminal transition (row `TerminatedAt`) in its one home, the SPEC-1 budget sentence; CODE-1 `terminalContext(ctx, terminatedAt)` gives the pipeline what remains of the budget on `s.clock`, with the whole budget for a zero `TerminatedAt`. SPEC-5 and SPEC-6 stay unchanged. — BECAUSE the stale slot release ages a member from `terminated_at`, which the mutator stamps before commit, so any lag between the stamp and the hook entry (commit latency, a late-confirmed commit under SPEC-5, writer work before the hook call) pushes an entry-anchored deadline past the §5.2 window. One count-from point closes every such lag, the late-confirmed write included. — ALTERNATIVES: a SPEC-5 qualifier ("runs only for what remains") is an exception clause in one row and leaves the ordinary commit-latency lag; widening the claimOrphanTimeout floor by the primary-read bound does not bound a commit stall, which runs on the writer's unbounded context; restating the count-from in SPEC-6 duplicates SPEC-1, which SPEC-6 already cites.
FACT: the tree already treats `terminated_at` as the start of the §5.2 post-terminal window: the watchdog stamps each row with its own clock read for exactly this reason. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/terminal_stamp_test.go:46-52; reaper cutoff pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/slotreaper/slotreaper.go:242; memstore skips a zero TerminatedAt pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/memstore/memstore.go:452.
FACT: `MarkTerminated` stamps TerminatedAt inside the mutator, keeps the first value on a relabel, and every terminal mutator calls it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/terminal_mark.go:20-47.
WATCHOUT: compute elapsed time on `s.clock`, never `time.Now`/`context.WithDeadline` against the row's absolute time: hook tests that run a fake server clock would otherwise get an expired context. A test that seeds `TerminatedAt` from a clock other than the server's will now see an expired pipeline context and has to seed consistently. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:81 (`clock func() time.Time`).
WATCHOUT: with an expired context, `releaseAfterTerminal` still takes the slot lock on `s.lifetime()` and the binder's claim and slot writes run detached, so a cut adapter Shutdown leaves the slot counted through a `leaked` member rather than acting on it. That is the existing cut-release outcome. Do not add a "skip the pipeline when the budget is spent" branch. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:103-112, 121-137.
WATCHOUT: `ReportAttachStreamFailure` bounds its own context from the report (seal window plus report timeout), not from a terminal transition. Its terminal write's hook re-bounds from `TerminatedAt` because `WithoutCancel` drops the outer deadline. SPEC-1's "the same bound" sentence names the duration. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/stream_failure.go:46-47.

UNVERIFIED: the SPEC-5 read-back on the primary can see a write that committed locally while the synchronous-replication wait was interrupted. If a failover then promotes a standby that never received that WAL, the writer has already run its post-commit steps for a write that no longer exists. The earlier pg_xact_status design had the same window. I judged this below the bar as general Postgres hardening and did not file it. A Postgres-HA reviewer should confirm whether §12.3's RPO=0 claim is meant to cover it.

FACT: The adapter `Shutdown` is issued inside `Binder.Release`, which `releaseAfterTerminal` calls after `drainMessagingOnTerminal`, the terminal events, and `terminalSignals` (cascadeOnFailure). So in the tree the drain, events, and cascade all happen before Shutdown. That puts it after Shutdown (13), FINAL_USAGE_REPORT (14), and session_end (15), which conflicts with §29.1 **Step numbering.** and with SPEC-1(a)'s pipeline order. Reported this round. The fix is a `gateway`, `internal` step placed right after the seal step, so do not move the sentence to step 16 again. EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:59-63, :722-763
FACT: Only `pgstore.Store.Update` issues `UPDATE sessions`. No other store method writes the row, so the SPEC-5 `last_write_id` read-back covers every session-row update path. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:491 (grep "UPDATE sessions" over pkg returns one hit)
DECISION: did not file the SPEC-5 case where a competing writer overwrites `last_write_id` before the read-back, so a committed write is misclassified — BECAUSE the row states that branch ("carries another value ... treats the write as not applied") and its terminal outcome, and it fails closed — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a mechanism defect (no applied text becomes wrong)

### [spec.6.review-performance.1]
FACT: The round-5 SPEC-5 rewrite (per-write `last_write_id` read back from the primary) adds no Postgres write: the identifier rides the existing sessions UPDATE, and the read-back runs only after a commit error, on a fresh pool connection after InTx released the failed one. Peak connection count during a failover is the same as in the shipped design. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:140; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:150-151 (RPO 0, synchronous replication, RTO < 30 s)
WATCHOUT: During a real failover (RTO up to 30 s) the read-back's primary-read bound (DefaultPrimaryReadTimeout, 5 s) usually expires first, so most lost-ack writes in that window are classified not applied. This is no worse than the shipped behaviour, where any Update error counts as not applied (F-7.2.29), and the SPEC-5 row and SPEC-4(a) state the outcome. Do not file it as a reliability regression. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/primary_read.go:12-16
FACT: The SPEC-6 slot floor does not move at default settings: claimOrphanTimeout defaults to 15 min, while the budget is 300 s seal plus a 30 s allowance, so the default meets the bound at every tier. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:591

### [spec.7.review-security.1]
FACT: The r6 to r7 spec diff has two changes: SPEC-1(a) now counts the terminal pipeline budget "from the session's terminal transition", and the SPEC-1(c) §29.4 renumbering yields steps 10 to 19 (new 11 is the terminal write, old 11 becomes the seal at 12, new 13 is the drain/cascade/terminal event, old 12-14 become 14-16, old 16-18 become 17-19). DECISION: no security finding this round. ALTERNATIVES: filed nothing on the SIGTERM wait "at most the terminal pipeline budget" (spec-changes.md:118). That wait is bounded by terminationGracePeriodSeconds whatever the counting origin, so it moves no bound.

### [spec.8.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: The orphan-GC mid-seal drain is closed by SPEC-6(b), a §4.6.1 predicate 1 conjunct: no session references the pod that is active or whose terminal transition is younger than `claimOrphanTimeout`. CODE-7 item 5 implements it as pgstore/memstore `PodHasLiveSession(ctx, podID, terminatedAfter)` plus a `terminatedAfter` cutoff on `warmpool.SessionLookup.PodHasActiveSession`, with a partial index. TEST-1 item 14 pins it. BECAUSE the pod GC aged from bindingStateTransitionTime, while the terminal-first hook seals after the terminal write (terminal_hook.go AfterTerminalWrite seals, then releases). The controller's only activeness source is GetActiveSlotsByPod, which excludes terminal rows (cmd/lenny-controller/main.go sessionActiveLookup; pgstore.go GetActiveSlotsByPod `state NOT IN (...)`). A session older than claimOrphanTimeout therefore had its pod drained at the first 60 s tick after the terminal commit. Keying on claimOrphanTimeout reuses the slot reaper's terminated_at age, so the SPEC-6(a) window bound covers both. ALTERNATIVES: a separate SPEC-8 (adds an index row and a checklist step for one clause); the gateway re-stamping bindingStateTransitionTime at the terminal write (redefines the §4.6.3 field, costs an apiserver write per session end, and fails open); reverting to seal-before-terminal (contradicts the tree and SPEC-1); an accepted departure (routine seal loss); applying the cutoff to every disposition (changes predicates 2 and 3); widening GetActiveSlotsByPod (inflates §5.2 rehydration and §12.4 fallback counts); adding the method to the sessionstore.Store interface (forces it onto countingStore and slowAncestorStore doubles).
WATCHOUT: §4.6.1 predicate 3 (unset binding state) carries the same phrase "and whose pod no active session references". SPEC-6(b) scopes its replacement to predicate 1, and a global replace would change predicate 3. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517 and :519
WATCHOUT: Leave GetActiveSlotsByPod unchanged. It seeds §5.2 rehydration and the §12.4 Redis-outage slot gate, and terminal rows would inflate slot occupancy. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:593; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:219
FACT: The existing index idx_sessions_active_by_pod covers non-terminal rows only (`WHERE pod_assignment <> '' AND state NOT IN (...)`), so the terminal-age half of the new query needs its own partial index. EVIDENCE: migrations/0080_sessions_active_by_pod_index.up.sql:22-25
FACT: The SessionLookup implementers are sessionActiveLookup (cmd/lenny-controller/main.go), stubSessions (gc_test.go), stubLookup (gc_internal_test.go), erroringLookup (gc_reclaim_internal_test.go), and orphanLookup (tests/tier8_chaos/session_suspend_resume_chaos_test.go). The controller builds the lookup from pgstore in cmd/lenny-controller/stores.go:54. EVIDENCE: lookup exact PodHasActiveSession
CORRECTS [spec.6.review-reliability.1]: its FACT that predicate 1 makes a terminal session left by a cut pipeline reclaimable at once no longer holds after SPEC-6(b). The pod becomes reclaimable `claimOrphanTimeout` after the terminal transition, which matches the slot reaper and leaves the SPEC-4(a) outcome sentence true.
DEFERRED [proposals/0100_fix_the-terminal-pipeline-what-happens-between-a-session-s/0100_fix_the-terminal-pipeline-what-happens-between-a-session-s.implementation-checklist.md]: S6 says "Lands the §5.2 claimOrphanTimeout sentence edit". SPEC-6 now also has part (b), the §4.6.1 predicate 1 edit, so S6 must land "the §5.2 claimOrphanTimeout sentence edit and the §4.6.1 predicate 1 edit". S14 (CODE-7) must also name the orphan-GC terminal-age lookup and its index migration. Tiers stay 0, 1, 2, and 7a, because item 14 runs at tiers 1 and 2.
DEFERRED [proposals/0100_fix_the-terminal-pipeline-what-happens-between-a-session-s/0100_fix_the-terminal-pipeline-what-happens-between-a-session-s.non-spec-changes.md]: docs/operator-guide/troubleshooting.md:355 (`SandboxClaimOrphanRateHigh` action) gives the drain condition as "and no active session uses the pod". After SPEC-6(b) it must read "and no session that is active, or that ended less than the claim orphan timeout ago, uses the pod". It belongs with the docs mirrors (configuration.md:520 for SPEC-6(a)) that the non-spec loop's docs deliverable owns, and this loop may not author that deliverable.
DECISION: The CODE-7 index row also gains `migrations/`, beyond the design's two packages, because item 5 adds an index migration and the row would otherwise omit a touched path. The summary's "one-site fixes" became "fixes" for the same reason: the orphan-GC lookup spans pgstore, memstore, gc.go, and main.go.

### [spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]
FACT: The orphan-GC drain mid-seal is live in the tree today, independent of the spec order: AfterTerminalWrite seals after the terminal write (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:66-86), a terminal write keeps pod_assignment (only failure.go:416,459, suspend_release.go:173,471, resume_rebind.go:209 clear it), the controller's activeness check is GetActiveSlotsByPod (cmd/lenny-controller/main.go:182-188, non-terminal only, pgstore.go:897-899), and reclaimByDraining drains the Sandbox and deletes the claim (pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:362-371). A `bound` claim's key is bindingStateTransitionTime, which nothing refreshes during a session. EVIDENCE: lines cited.
DECISION: fold the §4.6.1 predicate 1 edit into SPEC-6 as (b), and add CODE-7 item 5 plus TEST-1 item 14 — BECAUSE SPEC-6 already owns "claimOrphanTimeout exceeds the windows", and keying the pod GC on terminated_at (the slot reaper's key) makes that bound protect the pod with one knob — ALTERNATIVES: a separate SPEC-8 (another deliverable and checklist step for one clause); the gateway re-stamping bindingStateTransitionTime at the terminal write (redefines the §4.6.3 field as a non-transition stamp, adds an apiserver write per session end, fails open on apiserver loss); reverting to seal-before-terminal-write (contradicts the tree and SPEC-1); recording a departure (with a 60 s tick any session older than 15 min loses its seal with probability seal-duration/60 s).
DECISION: the code keeps `GetActiveSlotsByPod` unchanged and adds a store method on pgstore and memstore only, consumed through a one-method interface in cmd/lenny-controller — BECAUSE §5.2 rehydration seeds from GetActiveSlotsByPod, and putting the method on sessionstore.Store would force edits to the full-Store test doubles countingStore (delegation/service_test.go:415) and slowAncestorStore (mcptools/elicitation_perhop_internal_test.go:21), which implement every method with no embedding. The controller builds the lookup from sessionstorepg.New (cmd/lenny-controller/stores.go:54).
WATCHOUT: "and whose pod no active session references" occurs in BOTH §4.6.1 predicate 1 and predicate 3 (spec/04_system-components.md:517 and :519). The SPEC-6(b) anchor has to be scoped to the list item beginning "1. **Live binding states (`bound` or `recycling`).**", or the replacement lands twice.
WATCHOUT: ClaimGarbageCollector.evaluate runs the active-session lookup for every disposition, reserved included (gc.go:236), though spec predicate 2 names no such check. The new terminal-age cutoff must apply only to a bound/recycling claim (a zero cutoff otherwise), or predicates 2 and 3 silently gain a conjunct the spec does not state.
FACT: idx_sessions_active_by_pod (migrations/0080) covers non-terminal rows only, and no index covers terminal rows by pod_assignment or terminated_at, so the terminal-age query needs its own partial index. EVIDENCE: migrations/0080_sessions_active_by_pod_index.up.sql
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: S6 says "Lands the §5.2 claimOrphanTimeout sentence edit"; after this fix SPEC-6 also lands the §4.6.1 predicate 1 edit (SPEC-6(b)). S14 says CODE-7 "Lands the retention settle, resume-cleanup scope, fenced derive-failure insert, and terminate precondition"; CODE-7 now also lands the orphan-GC terminal-age lookup (item 5) and its index migration.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: docs/operator-guide/troubleshooting.md:355 says the controller drains an orphaned claim's pod once the last binding change is older than the claim orphan timeout "and no active session uses the pod"; after SPEC-6(b) it also requires that no session that ended less than the claim orphan timeout ago uses the pod. It belongs to the non-spec loop's docs deliverable, together with docs/operator-guide/configuration.md:520.

### [spec.8.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The orphan-claim GC (§4.6.1 predicate 1) ages a `bound`/`recycling` claim from its last binding-state transition, and its active-session check counts only non-terminal rows. Once the terminal write commits (terminal-first order SPEC-1 states), a claim bound longer than claimOrphanTimeout is a drain candidate on the next 60 s tick while the seal is still running. SPEC-6's window bound protects the stale slot release, which ages from terminatedAt, and does not protect the pod. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:236-251, cmd/lenny-controller/main.go:182-188, pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:897-899, spec/04_system-components.md:517

It only affects formatting, so I did not file it. — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.7 step 10/11 wrapped lines
FACT: The round-8 §29.4 renumbering is consistent. The new numbering is 10 client, 11 postgres, 12 seal, 13 drain/events, 14 Shutdown, 15 FINAL_USAGE_REPORT, 16 session_end, 17 lease, 18 release, 19 return. Step 10's "proceeds from step 11" still resolves. The only out-of-spec step-number citation is the code comment at pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:341, which says "step 15". That is non-spec, and the next loop owns it.

### [spec.8.review-performance.1]
FACT: Budget-vs-orphan math holds at defaults: terminal pipeline budget 300 s seal + 30 s allowance = 330 s, claimOrphanTimeout default 900 s, so the SPEC-6 ">= 1 s" bound is met with 570 s spare. Gateway grace is 240 s at Tier 1/2 and 300 s at Tier 3, both shorter than 330 s, which SPEC-4(a) states correctly for the default. EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:242; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:976; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:115
FACT: terminated_at is stamped on the writing gateway's clock inside the store mutator and kept on a relabel, so "budget counted from the terminal transition" (round-8 SPEC-1 edit) shares its clock and origin with the §5.2 stale slot release aging. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/terminal_mark.go:43; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:1078-1083
FACT: The staged design adds no per-session etcd, Redis hot-key, or Postgres write. The SPEC-5 unique write value rides on the existing session UPDATE, and its primary read fires only after a lost ack, bounded by DefaultPrimaryReadTimeout of 5 s. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/primary_read.go:12-16
DECISION: no findings under the performance and failure-mode lens in round 8 — BECAUSE a misclassified commit after a failover and a SIGKILL-cut pipeline both degrade to the claimOrphanTimeout backstop, which is no worse than the shipped behavior (F-7.2.29 and F-10.1.27 skip the tail today), and the 30 s allowance for the cascade matches the bound the shipped stream-failure report already uses — ALTERNATIVES: considered filing that a live replica keeps in-memory binding and leases after a misclassified commit, but that is a close variant of the fixed item "SPEC-5 row claims claimOrphanTimeout bounds a misclassified committed write".
