### [mechanism.post-terminal-window]

FACT: The window runs from the terminal commit to the end of AfterTerminalWrite and afterTerminalRelease. The hook seals, drains, emits, signals, and releases in that order, and a terminal write keeps pod_assignment. The rule's home is SPEC-1 (a), and the reasons for the edits are in D21. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:66-90; usage.go:331-334
FACT: §4.6.1 predicate 1 is covered by SPEC-6 (b) and CODE-7 item 5; see [spec.8.fix-G1.1].
FACT: §4.6.1 predicate 3 is unreachable during the window. claimer.go:216-221 and podregistry/crd.go:228-230 delete the claim and return the error when the first `bound` status patch fails, so no session row names a pod whose claim has an empty status. Predicate 2 is also unreachable, because a session in the window holds `bound` and `reserved` follows the release. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.6.1 **Orphaned `SandboxClaim` detection:**; gc.go:278-320
FACT: The §5.2 stale slot reaper is already spared. It selects only rows with terminated_at < now − terminalAge, and a zero value never matches. The terminal age equals claimOrphanTimeout, whose floor is gated at startup and re-based by SPEC-6 (a) and CODE-6. EVIDENCE: pgstore.go:936-952; cmd/lenny-gateway/startupgates.go:326-342
FACT: Sweeper sweepTerminal is guarded by the terminal claim record. ReleaseTerminalBinding returns while the writer's claim is open on the replica, and boundHere limits it to the binding replica. releaseMirror drops only the §10.1.8 mirror row, which the shutdown wait does not read. A binding on another replica is F-10.1.33, deferred. EVIDENCE: coordination.go:428-434, 528-533; terminal_binding.go:21-24, 47-50
FACT: Retention GC needs the CODE-7 item 1 floor for a tier window shorter than the budget; see D21. EVIDENCE: lifecycle.go:465; cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:1028; sessionserver.go:1793; leader_sweeps.go:117-134; retentiongc.go:263-270
FACT: Derive rule 1 is harmless for the window, because derive reads the source snapshot and writes only the derived session's chunks and intent row. SPEC-3 (b) folds in the `failed` bullet; see D13. EVIDENCE: derive.go:254, 415; terminal_hook.go:71-75; checkpointer.go:397
FACT: The §12.5 partial-manifest backstop needs a staged edit, for a seal row and for the row of a periodic checkpoint the sweep starts after the terminal commit; see D21. SPEC-6 (c), CODE-4 items 5 and 9, and TEST-1 item 15 address it. EVIDENCE: uploaddriver.go:347-348; checkpointer.go:287-295, 338-342, 439-455, 513-517; cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:364-411; partialmanifeststore/pgstore/pgstore.go:308-341, 393-406; cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go:552-566; spec/12_storage-architecture.md §12.5 Artifact Store
FACT: §12.8 user erasure, tenant suspension, and tenant deletion are an accepted exception to the window rule, and SPEC-1 (a) excepts them. Their drain does not wait for the pipeline, so the erasure step that follows can delete the row and checkpoints of a session still in its pipeline. The residual is F-12.8.43, deferred (summary, unstaged defects; D20). EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md §12.8 **Lifecycle fence and drain.**; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/drain.go:82
FACT: The §11.2.1 `session.completed` billing event is harmless. It is appended after the release on the bounded context, carries no state, and acts on nothing; a cut pipeline loses it under SPEC-4. EVIDENCE: usage.go:431-450
FACT: The REST terminate response is harmless. It returns the row its Update committed, which is the §15.1 "Resulting transition"; SPEC-2 (c) events report a relabel. EVIDENCE: session_transitions.go:95-103
FACT: The terminal events are already staged by SPEC-2 (c). EVIDENCE: terminal_hook.go:77-82
FACT: The other non-terminal readers are harmless. GetActiveSlotsByPod, ReserveSlotUnderLock, and ListPodsWithActiveSlots only count. After Redis loses a set, the seed omits a slot in its window for at most the budget, and occupancy-zero runs only on a release that removed its own member (slotcounter.go:13-18). PoolDrainStats only reports (pool_drain.go:112-121), and CountActiveSessions only gates admission (quota.go:293).
DECISION: PodHasLiveSession reads across tenants with InAllTenants — BECAUSE the controller holds only the claim's sandboxRef, the lookup it replaces already reads cross-tenant (main.go:182-188 to pgstore.go:889-905), §5.2 tenant pinning confines a pod's rows to one tenant, and the read returns one boolean — ALTERNATIVES: filtering by spec.tenantId, which adds a mismatch mode that drains a pod in use.
DECISION: a terminal row with a zero terminated_at does not count as live — BECAUSE every terminal mutator stamps TerminatedAt in its Update (terminal_mark.go:36-48), so a zero value never marks a pipeline in progress, and counting it would strand the pod because no age ever elapses — ALTERNATIVES: counting zero as live, which strands the pod and its claim. TEST-1 item 14 pins it.
FACT: The partial index on (pod_assignment, terminated_at) is needed, because idx_sessions_active_by_pod covers non-terminal rows only and the GC runs the lookup for every aged bound claim on each 60 s tick. EVIDENCE: migrations/0080_sessions_active_by_pod_index.up.sql:22-25; gc.go:236

### [redesign.1]

DECISION: Redesign 1 (post-terminal window) applied in full; every anchor of its ordered edit list matched exactly once and no edit was skipped. It added the post-terminal window to SPEC-1 (a), merged the derive rule 1 `failed` bullet in SPEC-3 (b), added SPEC-6 (c) (§10.1.7 intent-row deadlines for a seal upload and every other upload), CODE-4 item 9 and the item 5 sweep skip, the CODE-7 item 1 retention floor, TEST-1 item 15 and the item 9 and 10 cases, D21, and the [mechanism.post-terminal-window] ledger. BECAUSE the §12.5 partial-manifest backstop deletes the chunks of a live seal and of a periodic checkpoint started after the terminal commit, retention GC under a tier window shorter than the budget collects a live seal's chunks, and the `failed` bullet contradicts the tree (reasons in D13 and D21).
DECISION: Deleted with the redesign: the derive `failed` bullet (SPEC-3 (b)), the Open review-log item on it, the Deferred checklist items for S6 and S14 and the DOC-1 half of the DOC-1 step item (settled by the S6, S14, and S16 rewrites), the checklist's descriptive S6, S11, and S14 text, and the REC-1 phrase "derive narrowing". No open owner decision was recorded; D12 stays confirmed.
WATCHOUT: Replaced mechanism: derive rule 1's separate `failed` bullet ("uses the last successful checkpoint snapshot").
WATCHOUT: It lost because the hook seals every terminal session whenever a sealer is wired and derive copies whichever snapshot the row records, so the bullet contradicted the tree.
WATCHOUT: The alternative, forcing the checkpoint for a `failed` source in code, discards a sealed workspace and adds code; aligning the spec is the smaller change (D13).
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce a per-state derive bullet; derive rule 1 is one sealed-else-checkpoint rule for every terminal state.
WATCHOUT: Replaced mechanism: a retention GC window equal to `retentionForTier` alone, with no lower bound.
WATCHOUT: It lost because `--session-artifact-retention-seconds` has no lower bound, so a T3 or default-tier window below the terminal pipeline budget collects a live seal's chunks under the D18 predicate.
WATCHOUT: The rejected alternative is a startup gate refusing a window shorter than the budget; it adds a configuration rule the spec does not state and refuses a deployment the floor serves (D21).
WATCHOUT: Keep the floor in `RetentionWindow` (CODE-7 item 1); do not move it into a flag validator.
WATCHOUT: Replaced mechanism: an intent row whose `checkpoint_timeout_at` equals `checkpoint_started_at` because production leaves `Checkpointer.Deadline` zero, and a periodic attempt on the `Run` context with no deadline.
WATCHOUT: It lost because the §12.5 backstop selects such an `in_progress` row of a terminal session at once, and `ConfirmChunk` and `Finalise` return nil on the soft-deleted row, so the seal records a sealed snapshot over deleted chunks (D21).
WATCHOUT: SPEC-6 (c) and CODE-4 item 9 give every row the deadline its attempt runs under; CODE-4 item 5 stops the sweep at the seal start.
WATCHOUT: The §10.1.7 edit belongs to SPEC-6 (c), which holds the post-terminal-window consumers and already depends on S1; do not move it to SPEC-4.
OPEN: After SPEC-6 (c), a seal or periodic upload can finalise on a deadline fire (`manifest_reason = 'timeout'`), but §4.4.3 **Checkpoint abort cleanup:** classifies a deadline-fire finalisation only "on a drain, preStop, or barrier path" (retained for resume) and lists the other arms as swept. A deadline fire on a seal or periodic path falls in neither class. Decide whether SPEC-6 (c) also edits that paragraph. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.4.3 **Checkpoint abort cleanup:**; spec/10_gateway-internals.md §10.1.7 step 3 **Finalisation.**
OPEN: SPEC-6 (c) gives a seal upload's row the end of the seal retry window, while §4.4.3 **Checkpoint timeout and recovery:**, §28.5.1 `CH-CHECKPOINT` **Timing.**, and §29.5 step 4 state a 60-second timeout on every checkpoint path. The row deadline is a backstop bound at or after the attempt's end, so the texts do not contradict, but CODE-4 item 9 then runs a seal attempt with no 60 s cut. Confirm that reading. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.4.3; spec/28_communication-channels.md §28.5.1; spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.5
FACT: The related-passages lookup for the four changed statements (derive `failed` bullet, §10.1.7 intent-row deadline, SPEC-1 (a) window, retention floor) found no further spec or docs site the staged edits leave wrong beyond the two OPEN items above. §15.1 derive row and replay row, §7.2 mid-resume collapse, §12.5 backstop intent sentence, and §10.1.7 **Partial manifest on checkpoint timeout:** stay true. The docs derive table `failed` row (docs/client-guide/session-lifecycle.md **Source session state rules:**) is falsified and stays under the existing derive-table Deferred item, as the redesign records.

### [spec.9.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the Redis-loss slot seed (§5.2 **Post-recovery rehydration atomicity.**) and the §12.4 outage fallback are recorded as an exception in SPEC-1 (a), and D19 gains (d) — BECAUSE pod_assignment survives the release, so a seed that also counted young terminal rows would re-add members whose release already ran, and the reaper never removes a pod's last member, so they pin pods — ALTERNATIVES: staging §5.2/§12.4 plus GetActiveSlotsByPod/ReserveSlotUnderLock/ListPodsWithActiveSlots changes (three SQL predicates, memstore, migration, pinning); counting rows younger than claimOrphanTimeout (under-admits 900 s, mixes orphan-GC predicate into the capacity gate); a new SPEC-6 part in §5.2 (the window rule lives in SPEC-1 (a)).
WATCHOUT: the departure sentence sits immediately before "A terminal write runs on the writer's own context" and says "excepted" (no "also"), because G2 deletes the §12.8 exception sentence before it. Do not re-anchor it on the §12.8 sentence. The consequence named is both over-admission by one and the occupancy-zero disposition running mid-seal; keep both. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:593 ("non-terminal sessions"), spec/12_storage-architecture.md:219.

### [spec.9.fix-design-G1.1]

DECISION: Redis-loss slot seed and outage fallback are recorded as a departure in SPEC-1 (a) (one sentence after the §12.8 exception) plus a D19 (d) item, with no §5.2/§12.4 or GetActiveSlotsByPod change — BECAUSE the tree's terminal write keeps pod_assignment and the release does not clear it, so a seed that also counts terminal rows younger than the budget re-adds members whose release already ran; the stale reaper never removes a pod's last member, so those members pin pods, and the change touches three SQL predicates, memstore, an index migration, and the outage-end selection — ALTERNATIVES: staging §5.2/§12.4 to count terminal sessions inside the budget (rejected for the reasons above and the directive's departures-over-new-rules preference).
CORRECTS [mechanism.post-terminal-window]: the FACT "The other non-terminal readers are harmless ... occupancy-zero runs only on a release that removed its own member" is wrong for the seed. After a Redis loss the seed omits a sealing session's slot, so another session's release removes its own member, empties the set, and runs the occupancy-zero disposition while the seal still uses the pod. The pod can also admit one session over maxConcurrentSessions until the release. It is a departure, stated in SPEC-1 (a). EVIDENCE: pgstore.go GetActiveSlotsByPod and ReserveSlotUnderLock (state NOT IN terminal); slotcounter.go:13-18; spec/05 §5.2 **Slot assignment atomicity** ("A clean release that finds no member for the session changes nothing").
FACT: Every non-seal checkpoint path reaches Checkpointer.CheckpointWithTrigger: the periodic Sweep (via Checkpoint), the barrier dispatchOne (barrier.go:223), and the preStop tier-cap loop (prestop/checkpoint_adapter.go:38). Seal calls snapshot directly with source sealed. So one skip in the snapshot/driveCheckpoint path covers all three. The agent-pod eviction-signal path (§4.6.1 **Disruption protection for agent pods:**) has no gateway caller of TriggerEviction in the tree. EVIDENCE: checkpointer.go:338-375; grep TriggerEviction pkg cmd.
FACT: In the tree, an eviction or barrier checkpoint that arrives during a seal waits on the per-session lockFlight and then runs, recording WorkspaceSnapshot{Source: checkpoint} over the sealed snapshot. It does not supersede the seal's row while that row is in progress, because the single-flight lock serialises them. EVIDENCE: checkpointer.go driveCheckpoint lockFlight, snapshot Update closure.
DECISION: The closing check runs after lockFlight returns, for any source other than sealed, and returns the existing ErrNoBinding — BECAUSE a check before the lock passes for an attempt that then queues behind the seal, which is the discriminating case — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the Sweep-only skip (misses barrier and preStop); a guard in the snapshot Update that refuses to overwrite a sealed snapshot (leaves a wasted upload and the spec gap, hair); excluding sealing sessions from the barrier-target set (falsifies §10.1.8 step 1, §29.7 steps 3-4, and the CH-BARRIER card, and needs a new barrier seam).
WATCHOUT: A barrier sent to a sealing session now gets no Checkpoint stream, so that adapter holds quiescence until checkpointBarrierAckTimeoutSeconds and acks with an empty checkpoint_ref. That stays inside the grace term already budgeted for the barrier, and in the tree the same target already waits on the seal lock up to that deadline. Do not add a barrier-send skip without staging §10.1.8 step 1. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/coordination.go CheckpointBarrier select on done/ctx.
WATCHOUT: §10.1.8 step 3 ("opens the Checkpoint stream for each quiesced session"), §29.7 step 6, §4.6.1 **Disruption protection for agent pods:**, and §28.5.2 CH-ADAPTEREVENTS describe the drain in general. SPEC-1 (a) names the preStop and CheckpointBarrier checkpoints as the exception, in the window rule's one home. If a later lens files these as contradictions, answer with SPEC-1 (a), as the settled CH-ATTACH Degradation sites were answered. Do not add a qualifier at each site.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: docs/operator-guide/disaster-recovery.md item 4 (**Slot sets**), the paragraph beginning "After a Redis restart that loses the data": it lists the slots the rebuild drops (leaked slots, and a slot reserved before the session's record named the pod), and after SPEC-1 (a)'s Redis departure it also drops the slot of a session in its terminal pipeline. Until that session's release, the pod can run one session more than `maxConcurrentSessions`, and another session's release can free the pod. A docs deliverable must add this case without a spec citation.

### [spec.9.fix-design-G3.1]

DECISION: Every deadline fire (`manifest_reason = 'timeout'`) is retained, whatever the trigger. New SPEC-6 (d) deletes "on a drain, preStop, or barrier path" from §4.4.3 **Checkpoint abort cleanup:**, from the §28.5.1 `CH-CHECKPOINT` **Degradation.** bullet, and from the §28.8 `CH-CHECKPOINT` row. It also corrects the §4.4.3 reclaim clause and the §4.4.2 periodic example — BECAUSE the tree already retains every timeout row (uploaddriver.go:910, `reason != ReasonTimeout` gates the sweep), §28.5.5 and the §28.8 `CH-OBJSTORE` row already say "a deadline fire retains", and a seal row is on a terminal session, which the §12.5 backstop's `terminal_state` arm reclaims on its next cycle — ALTERNATIVES: adding seal and periodic deadline fires to the swept arms. That needs a trigger-conditioned branch in finaliseAbort, a new CODE item, and edits to §28.5.5 and the §28.8 CH-OBJSTORE row, and it buys one GC cycle for a seal row. Splitting the two (sweep seal rows, retain periodic rows) is an exception clause and was rejected as hair.
FACT: Derive selects the parent manifest by `WorkspaceSnapshot.Ref` (derive.go:476-478), not by the newest active row, so a retained seal `timeout` row is never what derive copies. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:466-478
FACT: A retained periodic `timeout` row can win a crash resume, because Reassembly on resume takes the newest active row at MAX(coordination_generation), gated by `partialRecoveryThresholdFraction`. This is the partial-recovery design the drain path already accepts, and the tree already does it. The next periodic attempt or the seal supersedes the row (Supersede-on-write applies "regardless of trigger"). EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md §10.1.7 **Reassembly on resume.** and **Supersede-on-write for repeated drain-timeouts.**
WATCHOUT: The §4.4.3 reclaim clause "reclaimed only by the resume-time cleanup ... or by the §12.5 backstop after `maxResumeWindowSeconds`" is false for the new arms. A seal row is backstop-swept at once (terminal_state arm), and a periodic row is superseded by the next attempt. SPEC-6 (d) replaces the clause. Do not leave it in place when deleting the qualifier. ### [spec.9.review-security.2]

USEFUL [spec.9.review-security.1]: its erasure trace re-verified and filed: DrainSessions skips rows already terminal (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/drain.go:91-93), ForceTerminate runs onTerminal synchronously only for rows it transitions (cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go:98-103), and DeleteByUser deletes MinIO artifacts at step 7, long before the SessionStore delete (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:839-852). ### [owner-delegate-2026-10-25]

- DECISION: the post-terminal window's end is a durable release marker written by the release, with TerminatedAt plus budget as a crash cap only; one store predicate with one bound and one zero-value rule replaces the four disagreeing heuristics (retention floor, PodHasLiveSession, the erasure and tenant-deletion gate, the slot reaper) and removes the Redis-seed departure where the marker makes it unnecessary. A column and migration are allowed. Chosen over a shared predicate on TerminatedAt alone because the release, not a timer, is what frees the resources, and a timer cannot see a release that finished early or a pipeline that is still running past an estimate.

REPLACED: retention floor (RetentionWindow = max(tier, budget)). LOST: nothing that holds; a seal needs a binding, so a row with no pod has no chunks in use. WHY: see D22.
REPLACED: PodHasLiveSession(terminatedAfter), its (pod_assignment, terminated_at) index, the SessionLookup signature change, and four fake edits. LOST: independence from a release-path write; a lost marker falls to the cap, which at the controller is still claimOrphanTimeout. WHY: see D22.
REPLACED: D19 (d) and the SPEC-1 (a) Redis-loss exception (over-admission and mid-seal occupancy-zero). LOST: a release whose marker misses after its SREM is re-seeded until the cap, and a re-seeded last member waits for the orphaned-claim drain, the residue a crash leaves today. WHY: the departure existed only because no row recorded the release.
REPLACED: the "cannot observe the end of the pipeline, wait for the step deadline" rule, SPEC-1 (e)'s pointer to it, and the TerminatedAt-age lifecycle gate the spec.9 DEFERREDs specified. WHY: pre-migration terminal rows and rows whose release records nothing stay NULL forever, so that index and the DISTINCT scan of ListPodsWithActiveSlots grow with history; the kept 0080 index plus a terminated_at-led terminal-arm index bound the scan to recent rows.

DECISION: the controller caps at claimOrphanTimeout, BECAUSE D22. ALTERNATIVES: a controller budget flag (a value below the gateway budget drains mid-pipeline); a cap persisted on the row (stamping at every terminal write). Open decision for the owner's delegate.
DECISION: the erasure gate is a runner hook at the head of runStoreDeleting, and a pending release leaves the job in progress with no failure, BECAUSE CODE-7 item 6 (b). ALTERNATIVES: the check inside the erasure drain hook, which fails the job and misses a resumed job.

DECISION: Redesign 2 (post-terminal window end and lifecycle store-deletion gate) applied in full; every anchor of its ordered edit list matched exactly once and no edit was skipped. It split SPEC-1 (a) into **Terminal pipeline context:**, **Post-terminal window:** (rules 1 to 3), **Gateway-owned terminal writes:**, and **Delivery and checkpoints during the seal:**; rewrote SPEC-1 (d) and (e); extended SPEC-6 (a) to the §5.2 stale-member release and rehydration seed and SPEC-6 (b) to predicates 1 and 3 and the pool-drain finalizer rule; added `TerminalPipelineBudgetFor` (CODE-1 item 1), the release marker and predicate (CODE-7 item 5), the lifecycle store-deletion gate (CODE-7 item 6), DOC-2, DOC-3, TEST-1 items 14 and 16, D22, and [mechanism.post-terminal-window-end]. WATCHOUT: It lost because a timer cannot see a release that finished early or a pipeline still running, and it was a second predicate beside the §5.2 seed (D22).
WATCHOUT: The controller now uses the unchanged `GetActiveSlotsByPod` on a store built with `WithPostTerminalCap(claimOrphanTimeout)`.
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce a controller-only lookup or change `SessionLookup`; the one predicate is CODE-7 item 5 (b).

WATCHOUT: They lost because the release marker gives retention GC and the §12.8 gate the actual end of the window, and a fixed age is a second bound.
WATCHOUT: Retention GC and the gate call `InPostTerminalWindow` with the cap from `gatewayWiring.postTerminalCap`, and fail closed when the cap is unset.
WATCHOUT: Do not move the §12.8 check into the erasure drain hook: a drain error fails the job, and a job resumed in `store_deleting` never re-runs the drain (CODE-7 item 6 (b)).
WATCHOUT: Replaced mechanism: the SPEC-1 (a) exception for the Redis-loss slot seed and the §12.4 outage gate, and D19 (d).
WATCHOUT: It lost because the departure existed only while no row recorded the release; with the marker, counting terminal rows no longer re-adds members whose release already ran.

WATCHOUT: Do not replace the 0080 index with a `pod_released_at IS NULL` index: pre-migration terminal rows and rows whose release records nothing stay NULL forever, so it grows with history.

### [spec.10.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the terminal pipeline budget is one deployment-wide number, defined in SPEC-1 (a) as "the largest `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` of any pool plus the release allowance" (in-place replace of the budget sentence) — BECAUSE every consumer of the budget (rule 1's crash cap, the store-side cap, the §12.8 gate, retention GC, the §10.1.7 shutdown wait, the §5.2 slot invariant) spans pools, and the largest value never ends a window during a slower pool's seal, which fails closed — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's appended conditional sentence ("a bound that spans sessions of more than one pool ... uses the largest"), rejected as an exception clause that also lists its consumers (a pointer describing other sites); rewriting §7.1 "(pool-level configuration, default: 300s)" to gateway-wide, rejected because it changes an unchanged configuration contract, falsifies docs/reference/configuration.md:132 and the §5.2 pool-admission check (spec/05:591), and is outside P2a; a per-session per-pool cap, rejected because the store-side predicate would need pool config per row.
FACT: the tree has no per-pool seal window. The only seal window is the gateway flag --workspace-seal-max-duration-seconds (sessionserver.sealMaxDuration, chart gateway.workspaceSealMaxDurationSeconds). So "the seal window in effect" in CODE-1 and CODE-7 item 5 (e) IS the largest of any pool for the tree as built — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:1167-1169, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:119, charts/lenny/values.yaml:2173-2181
WATCHOUT: do not delete the `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` name from SPEC-6 (a)'s §5.2 parenthetical to make it a bare pointer: the tier-11 test TestSlotOccupancyIsAKeyedSetAtEverySite_spec_5_2 requires that §5.2 sentence to name it — EVIDENCE: tests/tier11_docs/concurrent_slot_lifecycle_doc_reconciliation_test.go:417, summary.md:52
OPEN: if a per-pool maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds field is ever implemented, the CODE-7 item 5 (e) cap computed "once" at startup would go stale when pool admission accepts a pool with a larger seal window; not a P2a defect because the tree has one value.

### [spec.10.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode findings on the round-10 staged spec edits — BECAUSE the release marker adds no etcd write, watch, or reconcile load; the terminal-arm query is bounded by the terminated_at range (rows in their window plus never-marked rows newer than the cap); a lost marker (Postgres failover, crash) degrades to the cap, which is the durable fallback for every consumer (gateway seed and fallback at the budget, controller at claimOrphanTimeout, erasure and tenant deletion wait at most the budget); a Redis reset now seeds window sessions, which is stricter than the shipped seed — ALTERNATIVES: filing the §12.3 write-IOPS estimate and the SIGTERM-wait lease renewal, both rejected below.
FACT: Tier 3 sustained session creation is 200/s (spec/16_observability.md:596), so MarkPodReleased adds about 200 session-row UPDATEs/s at Tier 3 against the §12.3 estimate (session state ~300/s, total ~1,300/s, ceiling ~1,600/s, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:64-72, :125). Not filed: the ~300/s row already cannot cover create + running + terminal at 200 sessions/s, so the estimate is not per-write accounting, and the §12.3 instance-separation trigger takes the primary to ~400/s. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:130
OPEN: whether the §12.3 Tier 3 session-state estimate should count the release-marker write. A Postgres-sizing reviewer should decide; the remedy would touch §12.3, §16.5 PostgresWriteBurstIops, and §17.8.2, all of which repeat ~1,300/s.
FACT: The coordination Sweeper (renew plus adoption) runs on w.watchdogCtx, which is cancelled only when main returns, so renewal continues through httpSrv.Shutdown today and would continue through the SPEC-4(a) wait. Not filed: preStop step 10 already runs to the end of grace with renewal on, and SPEC-4(a) is bounded by the same grace, so the worst-case handoff delay equals the shipped one. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/runserver.go:212-230; cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go:269; cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go:42; spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.7 step 10
CORRECTS [spec.9.review-performance.1]: its lease-renewal finding against SPEC-4(a) "Only then does it stop its background work" fails the bar. Renewal and adoption already run during the preStop drain, which can last until the end of grace, so the added wait makes things no worse than the shipped worst case.
FACT: migrations use plain CREATE INDEX (0080), so the CODE-7 item 5 (a) index needs no CONCURRENTLY. EVIDENCE: migrations/0080_sessions_active_by_pod_index.up.sql:22
USEFUL [mechanism.post-terminal-window-end]: its inventory and REPLACED/LOST entries made the failure-mode trace (marker lost falls to cap) a check rather than a derivation.

### [spec.10.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: returned no findings — BECAUSE every recovery path traced through crash, restart, and store failover ends in a bounded reclaimer: a lost or failed release record falls to the cap (gateway budget, controller claimOrphanTimeout); a crash-cut seal's in_progress intent row is reclaimed by the §12.5 backstop once `now() > checkpoint_timeout_at` (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:363 predicate), which SPEC-6 (c) now sets for seal and other uploads; the erasure gate re-runs on a job resumed in store_deleting; tenant deletion Phase 2 retries on the next pass (spec/12:916 Phase 2 row; pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go:466 pausePhase) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the shutdown-wait predicate for not naming the release-record write (rejected: CODE-7 item 5 (d) places the write inside the claim, and a lost record only falls to the bounded cap).
FACT: maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds is "pool-level configuration" in spec (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:115) but a single gateway flag in code (cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:1169), so CODE-7 item 5 (e)'s single `postTerminalCap` matches the tree; pre-existing divergence, not filed. EVIDENCE: spec/07:115; cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:1169
FACT: the coordination lease stops renewing at the terminal transition and terminal rows are never adopted (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:28); sweepTerminal only hands a row to ReleaseTerminalBinding on the replica that binds it, and startRelease refuses while a writer claim is open, so no other replica releases a binding mid-pipeline and records the marker early. FACT: the DrainPool finalizer code keys on SandboxClaim phase (pkg/controller/sandbox/finalizer.go:116-127), so a crash-cut release leaves the finalizer held until orphaned-claim detection reclaims the claim; bounded, not filed.

### [spec.12.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no findings filed — BECAUSE the round-11 diff only widens the budget to "the largest maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds of any pool" (SPEC-1 (a), SPEC-6 (a)); the code has one deployment-wide --workspace-seal-max-duration-seconds (cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:1167) and no per-pool field in schemas/, so the max equals the flag and every consumer (store cap, shutdown wait, startup gate) gets one number — ALTERNATIVES: filing "budget recomputed when a pool with a larger seal window is admitted" rejected as hypothetical, since no per-pool seal field exists in the tree.
FACT: ReleaseTerminalBinding (the coordination Sweeper's remote release) does not seal; it only releases and returns leases, so a terminal write made on a replica that holds no binding gets no seal on the binding replica, and the remote release cannot extend past the window cap via a late seal — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:14-56
FACT: resume_pending and awaiting_client_action entry clear pod_assignment, so a terminate from resume_pending (SPEC-7) opens no post-terminal window on the failed pod — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:416, :459; resume_rebind.go:209
UNVERIFIED: a chunk PUT on a presigned grant minted just before the seal window ends can land up to checkpointCapabilityTTLSeconds later (spec/13_security-model.md:195, default 30). That equals the release allowance default (30 s), but the two are independently configurable, and a release that errors still writes the marker (non-spec CODE-7 item 5 (d)), so an object could land after the window ends and after user erasure's DeleteByUser. Security/erasure lens or the non-spec loop should check whether erasure tolerates stragglers.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-7 item 4 admits `StateResuming` in `EndpointTerminate`'s baseStates (as `EndpointDelete` already does) and makes `Validate` and `AllowedStates` echo only `AllStates()` members, admission still checking the unfiltered set — BECAUSE the echo is built in one place (session.go `Validate` and `AllowedStates`) and every serializer (sessionserver/error_envelope.go, mcptools.go, derive.go, mcptools_register.go, failure.go, session_terminal.go) reads it from there — ALTERNATIVES: a per-endpoint admitted-but-not-echoed field (the finding's suggestion), rejected because `EndpointDelete` admits `resuming` through baseStates and would keep leaking, or would need migrating, which gives two mechanisms for one rule; filtering in each serializer, rejected because there are six call sites.
FACT: DELETE already leaks `resuming` in details.allowedStates on the tree. `EndpointDelete` baseStates is `append(nonTerminalStates(), StateResuming)` and `Validate` copies baseStates into the error. The echo filter fixes this as a side effect, and DELETE from a terminal state is the TEST-1 item 12 case that fails on the pre-fix tree. A terminate-only 409 assertion would pass pre-fix. EVIDENCE: pkg/api/v1/session/session.go:334-343, :405-431; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:464

CODE-6 item 4 is the precedent — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's TEST-1 placements for the holds string, the terminator literals, and the migration tests (rejected for the same sequencing reason); reordering S15 before the code steps (rejected: TEST-1 cases fail on the pre-fix tree by design).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]

DECISION: lifecycle-harness coverage goes in a new TEST-1 item 17 (tiers 4 and 7a) rather than a bullet of item 14 — BECAUSE item 14 is framed "one case set per part of CODE-7 item 5" and the harness is no part of it; a new item leaves item 14's framing and tiers untouched — ALTERNATIVES: an item 14 bullet (forces an edit to its framing sentence and tier list).
DECISION: the harness checker keeps "required member = non-terminal row naming the pod" and widens only "permitted member / held pod" to the CODE-7 item 5 (b) holder predicate on the harness clock — BECAUSE a session whose marker write failed legitimately keeps a reseeded member until the cap, while a non-terminal session missing from its set is still a leak — ALTERNATIVES: replacing both directions with the holder predicate (would demand a member for an in-window row whose release already removed it).
WATCHOUT: the harness checker's quiescence Horizon is LeaseTTL+MaxResuming+MaxResumePending+3*SweepInterval = 125 s, below the default 330 s budget, so at Check time a row with no marker is still in its window; the scenario's direct marker assertions, not the checker, discriminate a missing marker — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness/checker.go:19, harness.go:50-56.
WATCHOUT: pgstore has no clock seam and stamps from time.Now (pgstore.go:166, :573), while SteppedClockStore stamps rows from the harness's stepped clock; a post-terminal cutoff computed in pgstore from the wall clock keeps an unmarked row in its window for a whole PostgresStore scenario. Cap-expiry assertions belong on the memstore harness only — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness/steppedstore.go (SteppedClockStore doc), postgres.go:32.
FACT: the harness builds its default store with memstore.NewWithClock(h.Clock.Now) and PostgresStore with sessionpg.New(pg.Pool); both constructors take variadic Options, so WithPostTerminalCap needs no signature change — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness/harness.go:276, postgres.go:32, memstore.go:69, pgstore.go:66.
FACT: ReleaseTerminalBinding's deferral behind an open terminal claim is already pinned ("open terminal claim defers", pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding_internal_test.go:112); TEST-1 item 4's old (d) re-pinned existing server behavior in a package that cannot see it. The Checkpointer's own new behavior is calling ReleaseGone on every tick the row reads absent (CODE-5 item 3) — EVIDENCE: replica_records.go:118-126.
FACT: cmd/lenny-gateway tests already build a real sessionserver.Server (coordination_held_seams_test.go, suspension_wiring_test.go), so the ReleaseGone wiring test can drive a real server and observe DispositionFailed rather than match source text — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_held_seams_test.go.
FACT: supersede of a retained `timeout` row is already pinned (TestDriverSupersedeDoesNotDoubleCountRetainedTimeoutRow_spec_16_1, TestDriverSupersedesPriorAbortedAttempt_spec_10_1); the new behavior for TEST-1 item 15 is that a periodic attempt now reaches the deadline-fire arm (uploaddriver.go onStreamError, isDeadline / d.ctx DeadlineExceeded). Item 15's annotation already names 4.4.3, so the finding's "add a 4.4.3 annotation" needs no edit — EVIDENCE: uploaddriver_test.go:748, :869; uploaddriver.go:759-782.
WATCHOUT: TEST-1 item 17 adds tier 4; S15's tier list (0, 1, 2, 7a, 8 before G3) must carry 4 — EVIDENCE: implementation-checklist.md S15.

### [non-spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: The §16.5 capacity-tier table labels Tier 3 session creation as "200/s sustained" (spec/16_observability.md:596), and §12.3 repeats it as sustained (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:259). The 30/s figure comes from the Little's-law derived claim rate (spec/16_observability.md:616). The spec itself calls 200/s a burst only in the §17.8 HPA tables (spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1019-1021). Proposal 0094 weighed per-create costs at 200/s (0094 review-log:746,803). EVIDENCE: as cited.
WATCHOUT: Summary open decision 3 calls 200/s a "burst". That framing is the basis for rejecting the alternative. Any reframing has to address the §16.5 "sustained" label. EVIDENCE: summary.md open decision 3; spec/16_observability.md:596.
FACT: The slot-account lock in releaseAfterTerminal is keyed by session ID rather than pod, so the CODE-7 item 5 (d) marker write (5 s bound) under that lock does not serialize a pod's releases. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:122.
FACT: Suspension, pod failure, and resume rebind clear pod_assignment, so terminal rows that never get a release marker are rare, and the terminal-arm index range stays small outside a MinIO outage. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/suspend_release.go:173,471; failure.go:416,459; resume_rebind.go:209.
FACT: admin.DrainSessions lists the whole scope from the primary with no state filter and no limit (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/drain.go:83; pgstore.go:660-720). This load predates the proposal. ReleasePending adds one more listing per Phase 2 pass. Not filed.
FACT: retentionForTier does tenant and environment Gets per call (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/lifecycle.go:465-495). CODE-7 item 1 resolves it only for rows past RetentionExpiresAt with a non-zero TerminatedAt, so the cost per sweep is small. Not filed (closes the "tier-lookup cost per expired row" part of the RetentionFor design-review trap).

### [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]

Lookup was JEV UNAVAILABLE (HTTP 402); exact search plus grep/sed used throughout.

- FACT: the coordination lease Sweeper runs on `w.watchdogCtx` (cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go:41-42), which the runserver shutdown never cancels (`watchdogCancel` is a deferred call at main return, cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go:252-265; runserver.go:212-259 has no cancel). So during the CODE-2 wait this replica keeps renewing the leases it holds. This is not a new hazard, because §10.1.7 stage 3 already keeps the replica alive through the grace period. It answers the "UNVERIFIED lease renewal during wait" Open item: renewal continues, and no finding was filed. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/runserver.go:212-259, pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:1-20
- FACT: `sessionServer.Close()` only cancels the lifetime context (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:1895-1899). It releases no lease or binding. — EVIDENCE: same
- FACT: erasure jobs run only through `Runner.Poll` → `RunPending` → `runLogged` (pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner_sweep.go:26-60). The admin handler calls only `Start` (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go:173). So a gate that returns `ErrReleasePending` from `Run` never reaches an HTTP response. — EVIDENCE: same
- FACT: tenant-deletion Phase 2 errors wrap `errSessionsNotDrained` and route to `pausePhase` rather than `fail` (pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go:338-341, 466-467). This matches CODE-7 item 6 (c). — EVIDENCE: same
- FACT: `admin.DrainSessions` lists with `ListFilter{UserID, Primary: true}` and no state filter or limit (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/drain.go:83), so `ReleasePending` on the same filter sees terminal rows. — EVIDENCE: same
- FACT: every terminal mutator found (orphancleanup, watchdog expiries, transitions, deadlock fail, translators) calls `MarkTerminated`. `transitionTerminate` is wrapped at session_transitions.go:83. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/orphancleanup/orphancleanup.go:256, pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:1122
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: docs/operator-guide/configuration.md:520 still states that claimOrphanTimeoutSeconds must exceed `gateway.workspaceSealMaxDurationSeconds` alone, and charts/lenny/values.yaml:44-48 says the same. After CODE-6 (gateway gate seal+allowance; chart gate `$sealWindow`+30) both should name the seal window plus the release allowance (default 30 s). Filed as a finding this round; DOC-3 is the natural home.
- WATCHOUT: `driveCheckpoint` releases the single-flight lock before `snapshot` records the row (checkpointer.go:494-500 vs 448-480). A periodic attempt that finished its drive before the seal took the lock can still commit its `checkpoint` snapshot after the seal's `sealed` one. This is pre-existing, and CODE-4 item 5 only guards attempts that start after the mark. Not filed because it is speculative (it needs the store write to stall for the whole seal drive). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:420-500

### [non-spec.1.review-security.1]
- DECISION: no security findings filed in non-spec round 1 — BECAUSE every new bound (pod_released_at, TerminatedAt, the cap) is written by the gateway, every unwired cap or gate fails closed (retention GC refuses pod-naming terminal rows, ReleasePending returns ErrReleaseCapUnset, a nil erasure gate fails the job), and no CRD, RBAC, NetworkPolicy, or agent-pod surface changes — ALTERNATIVES: filing the 30 s presigned-grant straggler (below) was rejected as pre-existing and narrower than the bar.
- FACT: CODE-5's synthesized row {ID, TenantID, State: failed} has CoordinationGeneration 0. bindingStale(bindGen, 0) is false for any stamped binding, so ReleaseGone reaches releaseCurrentBinding and returns the credential leases — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:113-125, :146-148
- FACT: DrainSessions lists with ListFilter{UserID, Primary} and no state or limit, so ReleasePending's "same filter" sees terminal rows — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/drain.go:82; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:1184-1209
- FACT: a persistent erasure-gate error is surfaced by the existing ErasureJobOverdue alert, so "records no failure" hides no degraded state — EVIDENCE: pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:1135
- OPEN: a checkpoint PUT grant outlives its attempt by up to checkpointCapabilityTTLSeconds (default 30 s; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:173, spec/13_security-model.md:195). After a seal that times out with grants still outstanding, the release marker can open the §12.8 erasure gate before such a straggler PUT lands. SPEC-1 (a) rule 3 treats the prefix objects as in use only until the release. The hazard existed before 0100 (erasure had no wait at all) and is not filed. A compliance reviewer should decide whether DeleteByUser needs a straggler pass.

### [non-spec.2.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The post-terminal release still runs when the hook's budget context has already expired. Binder.Release writes the claim (recycling patch or retire delete) on a detached context, ReleaseSlot retries on a detached context, and the slot-account lock takes s.lifetime() rather than ctx. TEST-1 item 1's new already-expired case ("release runs once, PodReleasedAt recorded") therefore holds on the tree's release path — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/release.go:64-69,:72-118; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go:856-882; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:121-138
FACT: With an expired context, sealWorkspace records `timeout` through the sealSleep(ctx) false branch, which is not a permanent classification — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:119-155
WATCHOUT: On an expired budget context, failWorkspaceSeal's relabel Update runs on that same context (seal.go:213) and fails, so the committed state stays as written. Do not add a TEST-1 item 1 assertion that the expired case relabels `failed`. Only the disposition (CODE-4 item 8) and the marker (WithoutCancel) survive — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:189-219
FACT: A deadline fire finalises the intent row `timeout` and retains the row and its chunks. The next attempt's supersedePriorAttempts reclaims them unless the prior row is at a higher generation, so TEST-1 item 15's new periodic-cut clause matches the code — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/uploaddriver.go:757-789,:410-476
FACT: The lifecycle harness PostgresStore wraps pgstore in SteppedClockStore, and pgstore has no clock seam, so TEST-1 item 17's statement that the pgstore cutoff uses the wall clock while TerminatedAt uses the stepped clock holds. The PostgresStore constructor currently takes only `now`, so threading WithPostTerminalCap through it changes its signature — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness/postgres.go:22-34; tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness/steppedstore.go:30-55
FACT: WorkspaceSealStuck fires on outcome="timeout", so the alert only fires after the seal has ended. The DOC-5 item 4 runbook text, which says the gateway ends the seal and force-terminate has no effect, is consistent with that — EVIDENCE: pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:1379-1384

### [non-spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the Checkpointer's session-row read runs inside `driveCheckpoint` after `lockFlight` returns and before `binding.Adapter.Checkpoint(ctx)` opens the stream; CODE-5 item 2 is its one home, and CODE-4 item 7 and TEST-1 item 4 name no location — BECAUSE the same read feeds the intent row's coordinationGen, recoveryGen and baselineBytes, which must be read under the flight lock — ALTERNATIVES: read in `snapshot` before `driveCheckpoint` (reviewer's suggestion) moves it ahead of `lockFlight`, so an attempt queued behind a seal writes a stale baseline; two reads (existence check plus context read) breaks "one row read" and doubles primary reads per tick.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the Checkpointer's single row read (CODE-5 item 2) runs inside `driveCheckpoint` after `lockFlight` returns and before `binding.Adapter.Checkpoint(ctx)` opens the stream; CODE-4 item 7 drops "`Checkpointer.snapshot`'s" and TEST-1 item 4(a) asserts "opens no adapter `Checkpoint` stream" in place of "makes no `driveCheckpoint` call" — BECAUSE the read also feeds the intent row's `coordinationGen`/`recoveryGen`/`baselineBytes`, which today are read after the flight lock, so a periodic attempt queued behind another checkpoint freezes the baseline that attempt just recorded — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "snapshot calls sessionCheckpointContext before driveCheckpoint" (one edit, but moves the read in front of `lockFlight`, so an attempt waiting up to the seal window behind another flight writes a stale baseline and generation into its intent row).
WATCHOUT: do not move the session-row read in front of `lockFlight`. `baselineBytes` is the attempt's resume-threshold denominator and is meant to be the last successful checkpoint before *this* attempt — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/uploaddriver.go:219-229, checkpointer.go:498-505 (lockFlight), :535 (stream open), :558 (CheckpointStart sent), :565 (today's read, after the stream opens).
FACT: today `snapshot` makes no row read of its own; `sessionCheckpointContext` uses `c.Sessions.Get` (replica) and returns only a value, swallowing every error — EVIDENCE: checkpointer.go:422-428, :602-608. CODE-5 item 2's "returns that error" therefore implies a signature change to (sessionCheckpointContext, error).
FACT: Seal's error chain is snapshot `fmt.Errorf("checkpointer: checkpoint session %s: %w")` over driveCheckpoint's return, so errors.Is(err, sessionstore.ErrNotFound) holds as long as each layer wraps with %w; TEST-1 item 4(e) pins it with the real Seal — EVIDENCE: checkpointer.go:396-402, :433-435.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: close the S14/TEST-1 tier-8 gap by adding the tier-8 Redis-restart suite to TEST-1 item 14's file list for case (c), with its store built with `WithPostTerminalCap`, and by adding 8 to item 14's tier line. S14's tier line stays as it is. BECAUSE `.claude/rules/test-coverage.md:51` assigns datastore-loss recovery paths to tier 8. The lifecycle harness behind item 17 runs on miniredis (tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness/harness.go:17,123), so tier 8 is the only tier that runs a real Redis restart against real pgstore SQL. ALTERNATIVES: dropping 8 from S14 is wrong because S14 also edits tests/tier8_chaos/erasure_receipt_crash_test.go (CODE-7 item 6 (b) fixture list), so S14's build has to run tier 8 anyway. WATCHOUT: `newSlotChaos` builds `sessionpg.New(pg.Pool)` with no cap (tests/tier8_chaos/slot_counter_redis_outage_test.go:253), and `counter()` hands that store to the counter as both its SlotSource and its FallbackSource (:291). An uncapped pgstore leaves out the terminal arm (CODE-7 item 5 (c)), so a tier-8 case run without the cap passes on the pre-fix tree. I checked the existing restart subtests (:399-:485) by reading them, without running them: they appear to keep passing with a capped store, because each terminal session either gets released or ends up removed by the release itself. UNVERIFIED for TestRedisOutageMidRehydrationAndMidRebuild_spec_12_4 (:553). The implementor should run it with the capped store.
FACT: the tier-8 test helper `c.update` stamps TerminatedAt through `sessionstore.MarkTerminated(r, from, time.Now().UTC())` (slot_counter_redis_outage_test.go:339-350), so a row it writes terminal is inside the window under any positive cap, and pgstore's cutoff comes from the wall clock. EVIDENCE: slot_counter_redis_outage_test.go:343-345
OPEN: CODE-7 item 5 (c) cites §12.4 (Redis failure behavior) because the store is also the counter's fallback source (WithFallbackSource, :291). No TEST-1 item at any tier asserts that the fallback count during an outage includes a session in its post-terminal window. A later test-coverage lens could raise this as a separate missing-test finding. This round does not add it.

### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no findings under the performance and failure-mode lens — BECAUSE the marker write adds one row Update per session end (~30/s at the Tier 3 30 claims/s steady rate, about 10% of the ~300/s "Session state updates" row and about 2.3% of the ~1,300/s total, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:64-70), the terminal-arm queries stay small in steady state, and every store failure (Postgres failover, Redis reset, replica crash) degrades fail-closed with the cap as the bound — ALTERNATIVES: filing the terminal-arm index column order (terminated_at, pod_assignment), rejected; see the WATCHOUT.
WATCHOUT: the CODE-7 item 5 (a) index leads with terminated_at, so a per-pod lookup (GetActiveSlotsByPod, ReserveSlotUnderLock) scans every unreleased terminal row cluster-wide inside the cap rather than only that pod's rows. In steady state that is about 30/s x a pipeline of a few seconds, or a few hundred entries, which is under the §5.2 5 ms claim. Under a sustained MinIO outage it is about 30 x 330 = ~10k entries, which index quals still filter without heap fetches. (pod_assignment, terminated_at) would favour per-pod lookups but makes ListPodsWithActiveSlots scan every NULL-marker row ever written. This is a preference between workable designs, so do not file it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:889-905, migrations/0080 index comment, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:593
FACT: the controller orphan GC calls GetActiveSlotsByPod once per candidate claim per 60 s sweep, and only for claims past their orphan key. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:54,236
FACT: DrainSessions lists the whole scope (all states, primary, no LIMIT), so CODE-7 item 6's ReleasePending, by reusing that filter, sees terminal rows. The gate works, at a cost of one more full scope listing per erasure tick or tenant-deletion pass. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/drain.go:85-92, pgstore.go:660-699
FACT: plan assertions in tier 2 store tests use `SET LOCAL enable_seqscan = off`, so TEST-1 item 14 (c)'s "plan uses the two partial indexes" has a working pattern to follow. EVIDENCE: tests/tier2_component/stores/sessionstore_podless_suspended_test.go:88
USEFUL [standing context "Release clears no pod_assignment", "Orphan predicates"]: these confirmed the consumer inventory. A grep of pod_assignment readers in pkg/ and cmd/ found no consumer outside it.

### [non-spec.4.review-reliability.1]
- FACT: afterTerminalRelease runs after the release, so it runs after the CODE-7 (d) marker. Its session-log close-hook writes no object, because the gateway passes a nil body and MinIOStore.Put returns early when the body is empty. The SPEC-1 (e) erasure gate therefore cannot be raced by the stderr.log write. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:403, pkg/gateway/session/sessionlogstore/sessionlogstore.go:186-188
- FACT: CleanupAfterResume on the tree cleans the latest active row whatever its state or generation. CODE-7 item 2 narrows it to rows below resumedGen and widens nothing. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/partialcleanup.go:487-500
- FACT: the abort-path Finalise in uploaddriver runs on WithoutCancel(d.ctx) with cleanupTimeout. A periodic attempt cut by the CODE-4 item 9 60 s deadline still finalises its row `timeout`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/uploaddriver.go:871-873
- FACT: pgstore ReserveSlotUnderLock serialises on pg_advisory_xact_lock and has no FOR UPDATE, so CODE-7 (c)'s UNION ALL arms are legal in it. pgstore has no injectable clock, so "the store's clock" means the wall clock, which matches TEST-1 item 17. EVIDENCE: pgstore.go:1017-1027, :55
- FACT: the server normalises sealMaxDuration and streamFailureReportTimeout at construction, so terminalPipelineBudget never sees a zero seal window. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:1795-1801
- UNVERIFIED: the billing session_completed Append and the quota final checkpoint in afterTerminalRelease run after the marker and carry UserID. An erasure that passes the gate in that gap of a few milliseconds could pseudonymise billing before the Append. This race predates the proposal, which narrows it, and it was not filed. The security or data-lifecycle lens should confirm whether the verifying phase catches a late billing event. EVIDENCE: usage.go:427-451, pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner_phases.go:237-269
- WATCHOUT: when the budget context expires before failWorkspaceSeal, the relabel Update runs on the expired hook context and fails. The session then stays `completed` and its outcome is `timeout`. This happens on a row stamped more than the budget before hook entry, or when write-to-seal-start exceeds the 30 s allowance. It is the accepted "Failed relabel returns input row" behaviour, and was not filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:213-230

### [non-spec.4.review-security.1]
FACT: A `resuming` session's row names no pod until the resume commit lands: commitResume sets `r.PodAssignment = b.sandbox` inside the resuming→running mutator, and resumeTail.publish puts the binding in podRegistry only after that commit. A terminate that commits from `resuming` makes the commit guard miss (notLanded, ReleaseUncommitted), so no binding exists for the hook's seal and the post-terminal window's "row names a pod" scope leaves no unsealed export outside the §12.8 gate. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go:518-531, :629-665, :692-700
FACT: admin.DrainSessions and the CODE-7 item 6 ReleasePending listing use `ListFilter{UserID, Primary: true}` with no state filter and no limit, so the gate sees terminal rows of the whole scope. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/drain.go:83-85; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:1184-1209
FACT: A gate listing error that persists leaves an erasure job in `store_deleting` with no failure observation; ErasureJobOverdue (pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:1135) still surfaces it, so the wait is not a silent degraded state. EVIDENCE: pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:1127-1140
DECISION: returned no findings under the security lens — BECAUSE the marker writers, the cap, and both gates are gateway- or controller-owned and fail closed when unwired (ErrReleaseCapUnset, ErrReleaseGateUnwired, retention refusal with no cap), the Redis seed falls back to Postgres rows, and no agent-pod self-report bounds any window — ALTERNATIVES: filing the "marker written whatever the release's error" rule; rejected because SPEC-1 (a) rule 1 states the same ("once that release has returned"), the hook's release follows the seal, and the tree's seed already frees a terminal session's slot at once, so it is no regression.

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-5 item 2 states the placement as an instruction (give `sessionCheckpointContext` an error return; move its one call in `driveCheckpoint` from after the `CheckpointStart` send to after `lockFlight` returns and before `binding.Adapter.Checkpoint` opens the stream) in place of a relative clause describing the tree — BECAUSE the round-4 text described the target placement as if it were the tree's current state, so an implementor would change only the read and leave it after the stream opens — ALTERNATIVES: deleting the placement (rejected: TEST-1 item 4 (a)/(e) "opens no adapter Checkpoint stream" needs it); doing the read in `snapshot`/`CheckpointWithTrigger` before `driveCheckpoint` (rejected: round 4 settled the read under `lockFlight`, CODE-4 item 7 cites CODE-5 item 2).
FACT: In the tree `driveCheckpoint` runs in this order: `lockFlight`, ctx deadline/cancel derivation, `binding.Adapter.Checkpoint(ctx)`, `stream.Send(start)`, then `sessionCheckpointContext` (the only caller, and no test calls it directly). The helper returns `sessionCheckpointContext` with no error and reads through `c.Sessions.Get`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:502, :519, :545, :548, :602-608
WATCHOUT: In a staged item, write a placement the code does not yet have as an instruction ("move X to Y"), and never as a relative clause ("which X calls at Y"). A fixer reads a relative clause as a description of the tree. This defect came back twice in that form. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-5 item 2

### [non-spec.7.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the retention GC guard's window lookup reports a non-not-found tenant or environment `Get` error, `RetentionFor`/`RetentionWindow` return `(time.Duration, error)`, and `eligible` skips the row for that pass (CODE-7 item 1; TEST-1 item 9 gains the T2 tenant-Get and environment-Get failure cases and a not-found case) — BECAUSE `effectiveWorkspaceTier` drops every `Get` error and resolves "", which `TierRetentionDefault` maps to the deployer default (7 d), so one transient tenant-store error shortened a T2 guard from 90 d to 7 d and the GC could delete artifacts of a session whose roll was lost — ALTERNATIVES: returning the error from sweepTenant (Tick stops at the first tenant error and holds up every later tenant); falling back to the longest tier (T1 is indefinite, and it is a fixed constant the "No fixed GC settle constant" trap rejects); changing retentionForTier's signature (create and roll callers fail soft by design); treating not-found as an error (tenant deletion erases artifacts on its own path).
FACT: the tier lookup swallows Get errors at pkg/gateway/sessionserver/lifecycle.go:479-498 (`if t, err := s.tenants.Get(...); err == nil`); the tenant and environment pgstores map a missing row to `tenantstore.ErrNotFound` (tenantstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:272) and `environmentstore.ErrNotFound` (environmentstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:150); `s.environments` is an `environmentstore.Store` (sessionserver.go:323) — EVIDENCE: those lines.
WATCHOUT: do not edit D18 to add a store-failure clause; with the fail-closed lookup its "cannot shorten retention" holds, and a clause there would state the reason a second time — EVIDENCE: summary.md D18.
CORRECTS [Settled: Retention GC guard (F-7.1.28)]: `RetentionFor` no longer "returns `retentionForTier`"; it returns that window together with the first non-not-found tenant or environment Get error, and an error makes the row ineligible for the pass.

### [non-spec.7.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: a non-ErrNotFound session-row read error in `sessionCheckpointContext` now aborts the checkpoint (returned, no stream, no intent row) in CODE-5 item 2, and TEST-1 item 4 (c) asserts no stream and no intent row — BECAUSE the tree's zero-value fallback writes intent rows at coordination generation 0, which CODE-7 item 2's `record.CoordinationGeneration >= resumedGen` guard then deletes as residue while the attempt is live; §10.1.7 already defines the row's generation as the coordinator's fenced generation at INSERT time — ALTERNATIVES: a generation-0 exception in CleanupAfterResume (cannot tell live from residue, and gen-0 rows still lose to the MAX(coordination_generation) selector); a generation carried on BindResult (new field, second source, and a failed primary read predicts a failed Put anyway); a new TEST-1 item (item 4 (c) owns the case).
WATCHOUT: do not keep degrade-and-continue for any trigger (eviction, barrier, suspension); any intent row at generation 0 reopens F-7.3.32. Every caller already tolerates the error: Sweep OnError, barrier CheckpointErr, suspension retry, and the seal loop retries anything `isPermanentSealError` does not classify — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:588-609 (degrade doc comment and `return sessionCheckpointContext{}` on Get error); pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:62,139.
FACT: spec §10.1.8 rule 5 ("If the Postgres read itself fails ... falls back to the last successful periodic checkpoint") is about the barrier's intent-row read at deadline, a different read; it stays true under this change — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:195.

### [non-spec.7.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: RetentionFor and (*Server).RetentionWindow return (time.Duration, error); the error is a tenant or environment Get failure other than the store's ErrNotFound; `eligible` treats an error as ineligible for that pass, like a nil RetentionFor — BECAUSE retentionForTier drops every Get error and resolves "" (T3 default, 7 d), so a transient tenant-store error shortens a T2 guard from 90 d to 7 d and the GC deletes irreversibly — ALTERNATIVES: propagate the error out of sweepTenant like the re-read Get failure (rejected: Tick stops at the first failing tenant, so one tenant's persistent environment-store error starves every later tenant's deletions, including T4's 24 h ceiling); fall back to the longest tier window on error (rejected: T1 is indefinite, and it invents a ceiling); make retentionForTier itself return an error (rejected: its create and roll callers deliberately fail soft, "rather than failing the create path", and lifecycle_internal_test.go TestRetentionForTier_spec_12_9_1043 would churn for no gain).
FACT: effectiveWorkspaceTier (lifecycle.go) has one caller, retentionForTier, whose callers are create.go:276, create_and_start.go:332, and rollRetentionOnTerminal (lifecycle.go:441). Not-found sentinels: tenantstore.ErrNotFound (tenantstore.go:538), environmentstore.ErrNotFound (environmentstore.go:337). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/lifecycle.go:465-498
WATCHOUT: the environment Get error matters as much as the tenant one: a T1 tenant whose environment override is T2 resolves to the deployer default on an environment error, which also shortens the guard. The design covers both stores. EVIDENCE: lifecycle.go:487-495, tenantstore.go:369-374
OPEN: TierRetentionDefault returns (0, true) for T1 ("indefinite"), but retentionForTier's `fixed && d > 0` sends T1 to s.defaultRetention, so the create-time stamp and the CODE-7 guard both treat a T1 session as 7 d. This is pre-existing tree behaviour and not filed by this round. Who should check: a §12.9 review. EVIDENCE: lifecycle.go:467, tenantstore.go:370-371

### [non-spec.7.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: CODE-5 item 2 returns every session-row read error (not only ErrNotFound) before the stream opens, so no intent row is written at generation 0 — BECAUSE §10.1.7 defines the intent row's `coordination_generation` as "the coordinator's fenced generation at intent-row INSERT time", so the tree's degrade-to-zero already violates the spec, and deleting the producer of generation-0 rows closes the CODE-7 item 2 hole without touching the cleanup guard — ALTERNATIVES: CleanupAfterResume skipping generation-0 rows (exception clause; leaves 0 ambiguous between live write and crash residue, and gen-0 rows still lose the MAX(coordination_generation) resume selector); carrying the generation on BindResult as a fallback (new field; BindResult has none, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/bindrequest.go:156).
FACT: every CheckpointWithTrigger/Seal caller already tolerates an error: Sweep reports via OnError (checkpointer.go:338-344), barrier records CheckpointErr and still acks (barrier.go:219-236), suspension records a retry and releases nothing (suspend_release.go:150-160), and the seal loop retries any error isPermanentSealError does not classify (seal.go:62). EVIDENCE: those lines.
FACT: the degrade path buys little: the intent-row Put goes to the same Postgres primary that ReadPrimary reads, so a primary-read failure usually fails the Put too. EVIDENCE: checkpointer.go:595-609 doc comment states the degrade rationale.
WATCHOUT: the `c.Sessions == nil` early return in sessionCheckpointContext still yields generation 0; production always wires Sessions (cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:365), so this is test-only and was left alone. EVIDENCE: checkpointer.go:603-605.

### [non-spec.7.review-mechanism.1]
- FACT: CODE-5 item 2 as rewritten in round 6 matches the tree: driveCheckpoint runs lockFlight, then the Deadline/WithCancel wrap, then binding.Adapter.Checkpoint, then the CheckpointStart send, and only then sessionCheckpointContext (which reads c.Sessions.Get, the replica). Moving the read ahead of the stream open is a pure reorder. c.Sessions is a full sessionstore.Store, so ReadPrimary compiles, and pgstore and memstore both implement GetPrimary, so ReadPrimary takes no row lock per tick. - FACT: ReleaseGone's synthesized row {ID, TenantID, State: failed} reaches releaseTerminalBinding with CoordinationGeneration 0. bindingStale(bindGen, 0) is false unless bindGen is 0, so the release proceeds. A stamped binding is never evicted on this path. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:96-124
- FACT: The release paths that sit outside CODE-7 item 5 (d)'s writer list are rollbackClaim (the row is not persisted), releaseEarlierBinding (the row is in a recovery state and the function clears pod_assignment itself), and reclaimFinalizedPod (the failure write returned an error). Under CODE-3, reclaimFinalizedPod's row is terminal only when the read-back failed. With no marker its window ends at the cap, which errs toward holding the pod. I did not file it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/bind_rollback.go:114-120, resume_rebind.go:178-192, finalize.go:395-404

- WATCHOUT: ReleaseTerminalBinding's doc comment says "It writes no session row". After CODE-7 item 5 (d), recordPodRelease runs inside releaseTerminalBinding, so the comment becomes false, and CODE-7 does not name it. This is code-comment drift only and I did not file it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:38

### [non-spec.7.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no findings under the performance and failure-mode lens in non-spec round 7 — BECAUSE the only r6->r7 change (CODE-5 item 2 moving the row read ahead of the stream open and onto ReadPrimary) shifts one existing per-checkpoint row read from the replica pool to the primary, about 10,000 sessions / 600 s periodicCheckpointIntervalSeconds = ~17 primary reads/s at Tier 3, with no added write; during a primary failover the read errors (not ErrNotFound) and degrades as today, and the attempt would already block on the primary intent-row insert — ALTERNATIVES: filing the primary-read shift against §12.3 **Read replicas** (rejected: that paragraph is a "should" routing guideline for read-heavy queries, and lifecycle-correctness reads already go to the primary).
FACT: Checkpointer.Sweep checkpoints every binding sequentially (checkpointer.go:338-344), so per-attempt latency added under lockFlight (the 5 s DefaultPrimaryReadTimeout on a stalled primary) adds to the sweep's length; pre-existing structure, not filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:338-344, :502-548
FACT: §4.4.3 states "All checkpoint paths enforce a 60-second checkpoint timeout", which CODE-4 item 9's 60 s periodic-attempt deadline matches. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:261
FACT: §16.5 Tier 4 (2,000/s creation) exists above Tier 3; earlier performance rounds sized only Tier 3. The terminal-arm scan stays small in steady state at either tier; only a sustained MinIO outage grows it (in-window rows = end rate x 330 s). EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:596
USEFUL [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]: its terminal-arm index-order WATCHOUT and the DrainSessions full-listing FACT saved re-deriving both.

### [non-spec.11.review-performance.1]
FACT: The terminal-arm index (CODE-7 item 5 (a)) leads with terminated_at, so a per-pod query (GetActiveSlotsByPod, ReserveSlotUnderLock) range-scans every unreleased in-window terminal row across tenants and filters pod_assignment as a non-leading btree qual. At Tier 3 (about 30 ends/s sustained, 200/s burst; summary open decision 3) with markers written within seconds of the seal, that is hundreds of index entries per query, inside the §5.2 "< 5ms under normal Postgres load" figure. The scan grows only when marker writes fail, for example during a Postgres outage, up to rate x cap (330 s gateway, 900 s controller). Not filed. ### [non-spec-recheck.1.review-security.1]
- FACT: admin.DrainSessions lists the scope with ListFilter{UserID, Primary: true} and returns terminal rows too (it skips them in its loop), so CODE-7 item 6 (a) ReleasePending reusing that filter does see terminal rows in their window. - FACT: the erasure runner's blob and store deletion all happen in runStoreDeleting (DeleteByUser), so a gate placed before DeleteByUser covers every object a late seal could write. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner_phases.go:184-212
- FACT: the gateway's §5.2 startup gate checks --slot-reaper-terminal-age rather than claimOrphanTimeout. ALTERNATIVES: filing controller/gateway claimOrphanTimeout skew outside Helm. Rejected because it predates this proposal, and it risks data loss rather than security.

### [non-spec-recheck.3.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: closed "no test pins the marker on a release error" by rewording TEST-1 item 14 (d) in place: each call site sets PodReleasedAt both on a successful release and when an injected fault makes the release return an error; a slot-lock wait error in releaseTerminalBinding joins the set-nothing list — BECAUSE the faulted case catches an implementation that skips the marker on a release error, and the lock-wait case catches one that records on any error releaseTerminalBinding returns (both are wrapped fmt.Errorf errors) — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's appended sentence naming ReclaimClaimed/releaseExecutor/releaseCurrentBinding/executor.ReleaseSession (a second copy of CODE-7 item 5 (d)'s writer list, leaves over-recording unpinned); changing CODE-7 to skip the marker on release error (settled by security rounds, SPEC-1 (a) rule 1 "once that release has returned").

USEFUL [non-spec FACT at review-log:1868]: releaseTerminalBinding returns before releaseCurrentBinding on lock-wait error or stale eviction; confirmed against the tree and used for the negative case.
